Narrative Review Details Saudi Arabia Pediatric Respiratory Syncytial Virus Burden - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A comprehensive narrative review published in the Journal of Infection and Public Health highlights the significant burden of respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) in Saudi Arabia, particularly among young infants. The review, conducted by researchers Ammar A. Alabdali, Chu Guat Yeap, and Thamir A. Alandijany, synthesizes observational studies, hospital-based surveillance, molecular genotyping, and health-economic models to assess RSV's impact on pediatric respiratory health. The findings indicate that RSV is a major cause of hospital admissions, with detection rates ranging from 20 to 30 percent in general pediatric cohorts. In Riyadh, 28.9 percent of 1,429 screened children under five tested positive for RSV, accounting for 79.1 percent of all virus-positive samples. In an 885-patient tertiary cohort, 60.3 percent of confirmed cases required hospital admission, with 26.2 percent needing intensive care and an overall mortality rate of 2.4 percent. In Jazan, 31.8 percent of hospitalized cases were admitted to intensive care, with a mortality rate of 8.7 percent. The authors emphasize that variations in detection rates are linked to clinical severity and testing criteria rather than geographic differences. They also note that young age, especially under twelve months, is the primary determinant of severe disease and hospitalization. The review underscores the need for continued genomic tracking of RSV-B isolates, as monoclonal antibodies targeting specific epitopes are being developed for broader use. The study highlights the seasonal nature of RSV, with peak activity between December and January and lower circulation during the summer months (June through August).
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Wednesday, November 25, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Narrative Review Details Saudi Arabia Pediatric Respiratory Syncytial Virus Burden by November 23, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Narrative Review Details Saudi Arabia Pediatric Respiratory Syncytial Virus Burden Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A comprehensive narrative review published in the Journal of Infection and Public Health demonstrates that respiratory syncytial virus constitutes a major cause of pediatric respiratory morbidity and hospital admissions across Saudi Arabia, concentrating its clinical impact predominantly among young infants during the autumn and winter months. Conducted by researchers Ammar A. Basabrain, Alaa M. Alkinani, Nedaa A. Alabdali, Chu Guat Yeap, and Thamir A. Alandijany, the investigation synthesized observational investigations, hospital-based surveillance reports, molecular genotyping studies, and health-economic models to characterize the pathogen’s epidemiological burden, regional seasonal timing, and emerging prophylactic approaches within the Kingdom. By evaluating decades of published pediatric data alongside recent genomic and post-pandemic surveillance, the authors outlined how viral circulation affects acute healthcare utilization and examined how evolving monoclonal antibodies and maternal immunization regimens might alter future infant disease prevention. To assemble the evidence base, the review team executed two complementary semantic literature searches using the Elicit platform, focusing on pediatric populations aged eighteen years or younger within Saudi Arabia or studies containing extractable Saudi-specific subgroup data. Eligibility criteria required laboratory confirmation via polymerase chain reaction, antigen detection, serology, or viral culture, as well as an established observational, surveillance, systematic review, or meta-analysis study design featuring a sample size of at least ten confirmed cases. Case reports, small case series, and investigations lacking distinguishable pathogen-specific outcomes were excluded from the analysis. The authors manually screened publications, cross-checking extracted clinical settings, observation periods, diagnostic methods, seasonal patterns, and economic parameters directly against primary sources, opting for a narrative synthesis rather than statistical pooling due to substantial heterogeneity in regional study denominators, clinical case selection, and diagnostic protocols. The synthesized prevalence data reveal that respiratory syncytial virus is repeatedly identified across Saudi healthcare centers, though reported detection proportions vary widely depending on study design and patient selection. Across general pediatric respiratory cohorts in tertiary centers, detection proportions commonly ranged between twenty and thirty percent. For example, early surveillance in Riyadh among hospitalized children under five years of age detected the virus in 413 of 1,429 screened children, or 28.9 percent, accounting for 79.1 percent of all virus-positive samples in that cohort. Subsequent cohorts in Riyadh noted detections of 22.4 percent and 26.9 percent in symptomatic pediatric populations. Broader multi-year analyses spanning the Eastern Province observed positive findings in 26.3 percent of 1,279 tested patients, while a retrospective analysis across the Middle East and North Africa region noted a consistent baseline range between 20 percent and 25 percent in Saudi pediatric settings. Discover more Respiratory Syncytial Virus Find Lung Care Respiratory syncytial virus respiratory syncytial virus Check Symptoms Marked variations in detection rates were directly linked to clinical severity cohorts and testing denominators rather than genuine geographic boundaries. In selected populations admitted with acute bronchiolitis, detection rates rose substantially; one multi-year bronchiolitis cohort of 417 patients found the virus in 54.9 percent of cases, while another cohort examining 142 tested infants under two years identified positive results in 50.0 percent of screened infants, representing 53.8 percent of virus-positive samples. Conversely, studies examining broad or mixed-age populations with respiratory symptoms reported much lower proportions, including 10.1 percent among 834 tested individuals in Jeddah and 0.14 percent among 5,864 screened individuals in Taif, where influenza A accounted for 71.1 percent of viral detections. The authors emphasized that these contrasting percentages reflect differing clinical thresholds for laboratory testing and varying pediatric age criteria rather than intrinsic regional differences in viral transmission. Across all reviewed epidemiological investigations, young age emerged as the primary determinant of clinical severity and hospitalization. A regional systematic review highlighted that children under twelve months of age accounted for 68.6 percent of all pediatric cases, while a tertiary specialist hospital cohort in Riyadh documented that 77.7 percent of all virus-associated hospital admissions were concentrated in infants during the first six months of life. Although several analyses noted a higher proportion of detections among male infants, such as 59.6 percent in regional surveillance data, age remained the single most consistent demographic indicator of severe lower respiratory tract involvement. In addition to young age, underlying vulnerabilities including prematurity, chronic lung disease, bronchopulmonary dysplasia, and hemodynamically significant congenital heart disease were repeatedly documented among pediatric patients requiring critical-care admission or specialized respiratory support. Discover more Read Med Journals Study Biology Find Pediatricians The review detailed substantial healthcare resource utilization associated with pediatric infections, particularly within inpatient and intensive care units. In an 885-patient tertiary cohort in Riyadh, 60.3 percent of confirmed pediatric patients required hospital admission, with 26.2 percent of those admitted needing intensive care support, 13.1 percent requiring ventilatory assistance, and an observed overall mortality rate of 2.4 percent. In Jazan, among 195 hospitalized cases, 31.8 percent were admitted to intensive care, 40.0 percent received oxygen or ventilatory support, and the mortality rate reached 8.7 percent, with patients in intensive care experiencing a mean hospital stay of 12.13 days compared to 6.24 days for non-intensive care patients. In the Eastern Province, the mean inpatient hospital stay was 6.5 days, while median stays in Riyadh reached six days for ward admissions and five days in intensive care, illustrating prolonged critical-care demands generated by severe cases. Surveillance spanning multiple Saudi provinces demonstrated an established seasonal rhythm, with viral activity concentrating primarily between October and March and reaching peak intensity during December and January. Long-term surveillance in Riyadh documented lowest circulation during the summer months of June through August alongside inverse associations with ambient temperature and positive associations with relative humidity. Southwestern surveillance in Jazan likewise identified peak activity in December with 29.2 percent of cases, followed by January with 22.1 percent. However, the timing of onset and duration showed notable flexibility across individual seasons and regions, with certain cohorts detecting circulation starting as early as August or September and persisting into April, cautioning against the application of rigid, five-month temperate-climate definitions to national prophylaxis administration calendars. The historical seasonal patterns were substantially disrupted during the COVID-19 pandemic, followed by an altered resurgence of pediatric infections. During periods of rigorous non-pharmaceutical interventions and movement restrictions, national viral detection plummeted; in the Eastern Province, no viral infections were detected during the primary pandemic months and the subsequent winter, accompanied by a documented drop in pediatric lower respiratory tract admissions from roughly 400 annually to 201 in 2020. As public health restrictions eased, circulation rebounded with unusual timing, including an off-season August 2021 surge in the Eastern Province and an early resurgence in Riyadh starting in October 2022. Investigators attributed these temporary shifts to an accumulation of susceptible, immunologically naive infants during periods of suppressed viral transmission, though whether seasonal boundaries have permanently shifted remains an active surveillance question. Molecular surveillance from Riyadh, Jeddah, and other centers confirmed the active co-circulation and ongoing evolution of both viral subgroups, RSV-A and RSV-B. Longitudinal tracking showed shifting predominance between subgroups over time; earlier studies in Riyadh and Jeddah documented RSV-A frequencies of 77 percent and 64 percent, respectively, whereas winter surveillance during 2022 and 2023 in Riyadh identified a slight predominance of RSV-B. Genotyping revealed evolutionary transitions, including the replacement of older NA1 strains by ON1 genotypes harboring a 72-nucleotide duplication in the G-protein gene, while RSV-B isolates clustered within the BA lineage carrying a 60-nucleotide duplication. Detailed sequencing of the prefusion fusion protein identified conservation across primary antigenic sites but highlighted eight amino-acid substitutions in RSV-B isolates, including six within antigenic site II, emphasizing the need for continued genomic tracking as monoclonal antibodies targeting these specific epitopes enter broader use. Historically, passive prophylaxis in Saudi Arabia has relied on palivizumab, administered as monthly intramuscular injections throughout the circulation period to high-risk infants with prematurity, bronchopulmonary dysplasia, or congenital heart disease. An eight-season study in Riyadh involving 1,704 high-risk infants demonstrated infection rates ranging from 3.9 percent to 9.1 percent across different dosing schedules. Additionally, a local Phase II clinical study in 18 high-risk infants verified the tolerability and pharmacokinetic maintenance of an extended seven-dose regimen tailored for prolonged local transmission periods. However, because palivizumab is restricted to high-risk groups, it leaves the broader infant population unaddressed; in one tertiary bronchiolitis cohort, only 5.6 percent of admitted children had received prophylaxis, underscoring that targeted approaches cannot mitigate overall hospital burden. To evaluate broader infant protection, health economists developed a national decision-analytic model simulating universal administration of nirsevimab, a single-dose long-acting monoclonal antibody. International clinical trials had demonstrated roughly 70 to 80 percent efficacy against medically attended lower respiratory infection and 62 to 83 percent efficacy against hospitalization, with real-world effectiveness reaching 80.7 percent. Assuming 90 percent coverage across a birth cohort, the Saudi model estimated that universal administration could avert approximately 58 percent of hospitalizations, intensive care admissions, and mechanical ventilation events, as well as 53 percent of emergency and primary care visits. The model projected the prevention of 8 of 14 annual infant deaths and reductions in direct respiratory-related healthcare expenditures between 274 million and 343 million Saudi riyals. The authors underscored critical methodological caveats regarding these economic projections, noting that they relied strictly on trial-derived efficacy inputs rather than observed domestic effectiveness or actual vaccine uptake rates. The model evaluated reductions in healthcare resource expenditure without incorporating drug acquisition costs, administrative delivery expenses, comprehensive implementation investments, or local antibody-escape data. Furthermore, while emerging options such as the fixed-dose monoclonal antibody clesrovimab and maternal bivalent prefusion F vaccination demonstrated robust international trial efficacy, no clinical-effectiveness studies have yet been conducted within Saudi Arabian populations. The review concluded that translating emerging immunization tools into national policy will require coordinated surveillance to establish standardized case definitions, determine regional timing, monitor viral antigenic drift, and conduct prospective real-world cost-effectiveness analyses. 在未接触过抗体的严重感染成人中检测到 尼塞韦单抗 抗性 RSV-B 变种 - Bioengineer.org

Nirsevimab-Resistant RSV-B Variant Detected in Antibody-Naïve Adult with Severe Infection - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报在一名因严重感染住院的 64 岁免疫功能低下成人中发现了具有 F:K68Q 取代的 尼塞韦单抗 抗性 RSV-B 变体。该患者之前没有接触过单克隆抗体,是法国 ARF-RSV 研究的一部分,该研究涉及 12 个医院中心。该变异是通过对相隔 7 天收集的两份鼻咽拭子进行全 基因组 测序而检测到的,显示出在两个样本中持续占主导地位。该研究强调了这种耐药菌株在人群中持续存在和传播的潜力,尽管它承认其局限性,包括缺乏对患者分离株的直接中和测定以及耐药性起源的不确定性。研究结果强调需要采取积极主动的预防策略,特别是对于免疫功能低下的个体,因为 RSV 仍然是弱势成年人发病和住院的一个重要原因。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该事件于 2026 年 11 月 20 日报道,患者住院并在入院前 7 天出现症状。
地点与对象
该病例记录在法国一名 64 岁免疫功能低下的成年人身上,这是涉及 12 个医院中心的 ARF-RSV 研究的一部分。
事件
正如临床医生和研究人员在 ARF-RSV 研究中报告的那样,在患有严重感染的单克隆抗体初治成人中发现了具有 F:K68Q 取代的 尼塞韦单抗 耐药性 RSV-B 变体。
规模、影响与风险
经证实,该耐药变体在患者的呼吸道样本中持续存在超过 7 天,没有检测到其他已知的耐药替代。该研究强调了此类变异在人群中持续存在和传播的潜在风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究通过基因组测序鉴定了耐药变体,并指出了局限性,包括缺乏对患者分离株的直接表型中和测定,以及耐药性是从头出现还是预先存在的不确定性。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Nirsevimab-Resistant RSV-B Variant Detected in Antibody-Naïve Adult with Severe Infection - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A nirsevimab-resistant RSV-B variant with the F:K68Q substitution was identified in a 64-year-old immunocompromised adult hospitalized with severe infection. The patient, who had no prior monoclonal antibody exposure, was part of the ARF-RSV study in France, which involves 12 hospital centers. The variant was detected through whole-genome sequencing of two nasopharyngeal swabs collected seven days apart, showing persistent dominance in both samples. The study highlights the potential for such resistant strains to persist and transmit within human populations, though it acknowledges limitations, including the absence of direct neutralization assays on the patient isolate and uncertainty about the origin of the resistance. The findings underscore the need for proactive prevention strategies, especially for immunocompromised individuals, as RSV continues to be a significant cause of morbidity and hospitalization in vulnerable adults.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Wednesday, November 25, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Nirsevimab-Resistant RSV-B Variant Detected in Antibody-Naïve Adult with Severe Infection by November 20, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Nirsevimab-Resistant RSV-B Variant Detected in Antibody-Naïve Adult with Severe Infection Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A nirsevimab-resistant respiratory syncytial virus subtype B variant harboring a dominant F:K68Q substitution in the viral fusion protein was identified in a 64-year-old immunocompromised adult hospitalized with severe infection who had never received monoclonal antibody prophylaxis. The case was documented by clinicians and researchers investigating virological determinants of disease severity, demonstrating longitudinal persistence of the resistant strain across sequential respiratory samples collected during hospitalization. The patient was enrolled as part of the ongoing ARF-RSV study, registered under ClinicalTrials.gov identifier NCT06197152, which is an observational, prospective multicenter cohort conducted across 12 participating hospital centers in France. The primary objective of the cohort investigation is to characterize viral genomic diversity, identify biomarkers linked to respiratory syncytial virus infection severity in adult populations, and validate their potential application in clinical practice. Under the ARF-RSV protocol, adult patients hospitalized for acute respiratory infection with polymerase chain reaction-confirmed viral infection are prospectively evaluated. The study protocol defines severe illness as acute respiratory failure corresponding to a score of six or higher on the World Health Organization ten-point scale at any time during hospitalization, comparing those patients against individuals with mild disease whose scores remain below six. As of June 2026, the multicenter effort had enrolled 117 participants toward a planned recruitment target of 233 patients. Discover more Save on Rx Find Lung Care Track Biotech Study Biology Respiratory syncytial virus The patient described in the report had a recorded medical history of aggressive mantle cell lymphoma undergoing active management, insulin-dependent type 2 diabetes mellitus, and hypertension. One week before admission to an intensive care unit, he had received lymphodepletion chemotherapy in preparation for planned chimeric antigen receptor T-cell administration, and he had not received vaccination against respiratory syncytial virus prior to entering the cellular therapy program. Upon admission to the intensive care unit, the patient presented with severe sepsis in the setting of febrile aplasia and a respiratory syndrome that had been evolving for seven days prior to presentation. Clinical management required initial hemodynamic support consistent with septic shock, alongside empirical broad-spectrum antibiotic coverage for a suspected but undocumented concurrent bacterial infection. Hemodynamic failure resolved within a few days, and his planned chimeric antigen receptor T-cell infusion was temporarily suspended before being successfully administered one month later. Diagnostic microbiology confirmed respiratory syncytial virus infection in initial nasopharyngeal material, leading to whole-genome viral sequencing using an amplification framework adapted from the French National Influenza Reference Center protocol. Reference-based genome reconstruction and deep sequencing were performed on two sequential nasopharyngeal swabs collected seven days apart, both achieving complete one-hundred percent genome coverage with high sequencing depth. Genetic analysis determined that the infecting isolate belonged to lineage B.D.E.1 and harbored the F:K68Q substitution within the viral fusion protein in both specimens. In the primary swab, the sequencing run achieved an average coverage depth exceeding 180-fold, detecting the substitution at an allele frequency of 99 percent. The follow-up swab obtained seven days later exhibited an average coverage depth of 170-fold, with the substitution maintained at an allele frequency of 97 percent, confirming the persistence of the resistant variant as the dominant intrahost population over time. Discover more respiratory syncytial virus Check Symptoms Read Med Journals infection Infectious Diseases Beyond the primary K68Q change, consensus sequencing revealed the presence of R209Q, S190N, and S211N substitutions within the viral fusion protein. Deep sequencing identified no other minority substitutions known to confer resistance within the analyzed fusion protein sequences. The patient had received no prior doses of nirsevimab, establishing that the variant was present in a monoclonal antibody-naïve individual. The F:K68Q substitution has previously been documented to confer high-level resistance to nirsevimab neutralization. In published literature, independent phenotypic neutralization assays by Wilkins and colleagues demonstrated markedly diminished nirsevimab susceptibility in RSV-B carrying K68Q, while deep mutational scanning experiments by Simonich and colleagues independently verified that this specific change strongly impairs antibody neutralization. Global surveillance records from GISAID indicate that the substitution is rare among circulating strains, though historical clusters reported in Kenya in 2017 support its classification as an episodic, naturally occurring polymorphism. The authors highlighted several methodological considerations and study limitations, including the absence of direct phenotypic neutralization assays performed on the patient isolate itself. Additionally, because a seven-day interval elapsed between the onset of respiratory symptoms and hospital admission, investigators could not resolve whether the patient acquired an already-circulating resistant lineage or whether the F:K68Q substitution emerged de novo during replication within the host. The investigators concluded that the documented longitudinal persistence of a resistant viral variant in an antibody-naïve, immunocompromised patient indicates that such strains can persist and potentially transmit within human populations. They noted that prospective genomic surveillance within hospital cohorts remains essential for distinguishing baseline polymorphisms from selective escapes, while respiratory syncytial virus vaccination among immunocompromised groups could provide direct protection and mitigate community dissemination of resistant variants. Beyond the immediate acute presentation, respiratory syncytial virus has increasingly emerged as a major cause of serious clinical morbidity and hospitalization among vulnerable adults, especially individuals living with significant cardiorespiratory comorbidities or severe immunosuppression. Following an acute episode, patients frequently suffer from persistent, debilitating long-term symptoms, underscoring the urgent necessity of expanding proactive viral prevention strategies. In response to this ongoing burden, clinical practice guidelines published by the Infectious Diseases Society of America provide strong recommendations supporting respiratory syncytial virus vaccination for immunocompromised adolescents and adults, specifically highlighting individuals managing hematological malignancies. Concurrently, the recent development and clinical introduction of monoclonal antibodies directed against the viral prefusion fusion protein, including nirsevimab, has marked a substantial therapeutic advance in pediatric medicine by preventing severe disease in infants. Clinicians and researchers anticipate that similar antibody-based strategies targeting the prefusion fusion protein could eventually be utilized to prevent respiratory syncytial virus-related hospital admissions and critical illness in severely immunocompromised adult populations, although emerging alterations within the fusion protein present substantial challenges to this approach. The investigative protocol of the broader prospective cohort incorporates rigorous ethical, temporal, and biological parameters to evaluate disease severity mechanisms. Formal ethical oversight for the investigation was granted by the CPP Sud-Méditerranée I ethics committee on February 3, 2023, under reference number 2022-A02194-39, mandating that written informed consent be obtained from all participating patients or their designated legal representatives prior to data acquisition. Cohort recruitment officially initiated with the inclusion of the first enrolled patient on December 27, 2023. Beyond standard clinical surveillance, the multi-center protocol incorporates an extensive biological evaluation strategy designed to contrast inflammatory and immune pathways between severe and mild disease cohorts. Specifically, researchers perform detailed transcriptomic analyses using peripheral blood and respiratory specimens collected within seventy-two hours of hospital admission. These respiratory materials include routine nasopharyngeal swabs as well as invasive lower respiratory samples, such as bronchoalveolar lavage fluid or tracheal aspirates when clinically obtainable, ensuring comprehensive profiling of host antiviral defenses during early infection. Methodologically, the study authors emphasize that pairing prospective clinical observation with high-resolution genomic surveillance provides crucial clarity regarding viral evolution. In the clinical case, viral genomes were fully assembled and resolved through deep sequencing pipelines, permitting precise quantification of intrahost viral diversity and fine mapping of both dominant alleles and potential minority variants. Establishing whether an observed amino acid change represents an isolated primary polymorphism or an acquired selective escape is critical for interpreting therapeutic efficacy. Because the patient had no recorded prior exposure to monoclonal antibodies, the detection of the dominant F:K68Q variant reinforces the hypothesis that this specific resistant lineage can circulate entirely independently of drug-mediated pressure. Epidemiological evidence from historical respiratory syncytial virus outbreaks, such as previously identified cluster cases in Kenya dating back to 2017, provides concrete historical precedent that K68Q can behave as a naturally occurring polymorphism undergoing episodic expansion in the absence of therapeutic monoclonal antibody selection. The re-emergence of these resistant variants during an era characterized by expanding prophylactic antibody deployment introduces significant epidemiological concerns. When prophylactic agents such as nirsevimab achieve widespread community coverage, existing baseline variants carrying resistance substitutions may encounter selective advantages that facilitate their amplification and subsequent transmission. Within this evolutionary landscape, immunocompromised individuals occupy a uniquely vulnerable position. Because impaired immune systems struggle to achieve swift viral clearance, immunocompromised hosts can serve as substantial anatomical reservoirs supporting prolonged viral replication. This extended duration of replication provides an environment conducive to ongoing intrahost diversification, thereby creating opportunities for resistant variants to stabilize, persist over time, or emerge de novo. Tracking viral kinetics and genomic stability within immunocompromised populations is therefore essential for anticipating broader community transmission dynamics. In discussing the distinctive strengths of their investigation, the authors underscored that conducting the research within a structured, prospective multicenter study framework ensured rigorous sample collection within predefined clinical intervals. Rather than relying on opportunistic retrospective testing, patient evaluations and diagnostic samplings were executed under standardized prospective operational protocols. The systematic acquisition of paired sequential respiratory specimens, separated by a seven-day interval and evaluated via whole-genome deep sequencing, provided definitive molecular proof that the F:K68Q variant maintained absolute intrahost dominance over an extended timeframe. According to the study investigators, this longitudinal persistence of an escape variant carrying documented high-level resistance had not previously been observed or documented in human clinical literature, marking a notable milestone in understanding respiratory syncytial virus evolutionary behavior. Comprehensive Review Details Respiratory Syncytial Virus Biology, Epidemiology, and Approved Preventive Strategies - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报A comprehensive review published on November 24, 2026, in Tropical Diseases, Travel Medicine and Vaccines details the biology, epidemiology, and preventive strategies of Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV). The study highlights RSV's significant clinical impact, particularly on young infants and older adults in developed nations. In 2019, it infected approximately 33 million children under five globally, leading to 3.6 million hospital admissions and an estimated 101,400 pediatric deaths. Infants under six months experienced 6.6 million clinical episodes and 1.4 million hospitalizations, with nearly 45,700 attributable deaths. The majority of these cases and deaths occurred in low- and middle-income countries, underscoring global health disparities. The review outlines RSV's classification within the Mononegavirales order, Orthopneumovirus genus, and Pneumoviridae family, describing its enveloped structure and molecular organization. It emphasizes the virus's surface glycoproteins, G and F, which play key roles in immune evasion and pathogenesis. The study also notes that while ribavirin showed no significant mortality reduction in general hospitalized cohorts, it demonstrated profound reductions in specific subgroups, such as those with underlying hematological malignancies.

时间
该综述发表于2026年11月24日,引用的数据包括2019年以来的全球监测以及发达国家正在进行的研究。
地点与对象
该审查涵盖全球数据,重点关注低收入和中等收入国家,并包括发达国家五岁以下儿童和老年人的统计数据。
事件
发表在《热带疾病》、《旅行医学》和《疫苗》上的综合叙述性评论分析了 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 的生物学、流行病学 和预防策略,强调了其临床影响和分子结构。
规模、影响与风险
In 2019, RSV infected approximately 33 million children under five globally, resulting in 3.6 million hospital admissions and an estimated 101,400 pediatric deaths. In infants under six months, it caused 6.6 million clinical episodes and 1.4 million hospitalizations, with nearly 45,700 attributable deaths. Over 95% of acute lower respiratory tract infections and 97% of associated mortalities occurred in low- and middle-income countries.
应对、状态与不确定性
该综述对 RSV 的生物学和 流行病学 进行了详细分析,包括其在单链负病毒目、正肺病毒属和肺病毒科中的分类。它还讨论了病毒的结构特征、传播机制以及其表面糖蛋白在免疫逃避和发病机制中的作用。审查指出,虽然利巴韦林在一般住院 队列研究 中没有显示出显着的 死亡率 减少,但它对特定亚组(如患有血液恶性肿瘤的亚组)具有显着效果。

Comprehensive Review Details Respiratory Syncytial Virus Biology, Epidemiology, and Approved Preventive Strategies - Bioengineer.org

A comprehensive review published on November 24, 2026, in Tropical Diseases, Travel Medicine and Vaccines details the biology, epidemiology, and preventive strategies of Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV). The study highlights RSV's significant clinical impact, particularly on young infants and older adults in developed nations. In 2019, it infected approximately 33 million children under five globally, leading to 3.6 million hospital admissions and an estimated 101,400 pediatric deaths. Infants under six months experienced 6.6 million clinical episodes and 1.4 million hospitalizations, with nearly 45,700 attributable deaths. The majority of these cases and deaths occurred in low- and middle-income countries, underscoring global health disparities. The review outlines RSV's classification within the Mononegavirales order, Orthopneumovirus genus, and Pneumoviridae family, describing its enveloped structure and molecular organization. It emphasizes the virus's surface glycoproteins, G and F, which play key roles in immune evasion and pathogenesis. The study also notes that while ribavirin showed no significant mortality reduction in general hospitalized cohorts, it demonstrated profound reductions in specific subgroups, such as those with underlying hematological malignancies.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Wednesday, November 25, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Comprehensive Review Details Respiratory Syncytial Virus Biology, Epidemiology, and Approved Preventive Strategies by November 24, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 8 mins read 0 Comprehensive Review Details Respiratory Syncytial Virus Biology, Epidemiology, and Approved Preventive Strategies Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A comprehensive narrative review published in Tropical Diseases, Travel Medicine and Vaccines provides a detailed analysis of Respiratory Syncytial Virus, charting its pathogenic mechanisms, global epidemiologic footprint, molecular architecture, and the evolving landscape of modern clinical interventions. Globally recognized as a formidable respiratory pathogen, the virus exerts an immense clinical toll across vulnerable demographics, most notably young infants and medically frail older adults. Drawing on global surveillance syntheses, the authors highlight that in 2019 alone, the pathogen infected approximately 33 million children under the age of five worldwide. This staggering burden resulted in roughly 3.6 million hospital admissions and an estimated 101,400 pediatric fatalities. In infants under six months of age, acute lower respiratory tract infections driven by the virus caused approximately 6.6 million clinical episodes and 1.4 million hospitalizations, with total attributable deaths reaching nearly 45,700 within this narrow age bracket. Crucially, more than 95 percent of all related acute lower respiratory tract infections and over 97 percent of associated mortalities occurred within low- and middle-income countries, demonstrating stark international health disparities. Beyond pediatric populations, systematic assessments in developed nations establish that the virus accounts for 4.66 percent of symptomatic respiratory illnesses annually and 7.80 percent seasonally among older adults, carrying a substantial 8.18 percent case fatality rate that escalates to 9.88 percent among individuals with chronic cardiopulmonary comorbidities. From a structural and taxonomic standpoint, the pathogen is classified within the Mononegavirales order, the Orthopneumovirus genus, and the Pneumoviridae family. Transmission electron microscopy reveals that the virion is an enveloped, pleomorphic structure measuring between 0.5 and 12 micrometers in length with a cross-sectional diameter of roughly 130 nanometers. Enclosed within a host-derived lipid membrane resides a left-handed helical nucleocapsid that encapsulates the viral genome, which consists of a single-stranded, negative-sense ribonucleic acid molecule spanning exactly 15,222 nucleotides. This viral genome is systematically organized into ten discrete coding regions that collectively orchestrate the transcription and translation of eleven distinct viral proteins. Arranged linearly from the three-prime to five-prime orientation, the genomic sequence encodes two non-structural proteins designated NS1 and NS2, followed sequentially by the nucleocapsid protein, phosphoprotein, matrix protein, small hydrophobic protein, attachment glycoprotein, fusion glycoprotein, two regulatory M2 proteins derived from slightly overlapping open reading frames, and a large catalytic polymerase protein. Viral transcription proceeds sequentially to yield ten subgenomic messenger ribonucleic acids, each fitted with a five-prime cap and a three-prime polyadenylation tail, with the viral ribonucleic acid-dependent ribonucleic acid polymerase strictly regulated by conserved cis-acting gene-start and gene-end boundary motifs that govern initiation and termination. The viral envelope displays two predominant surface glycoproteins that govern cellular attachment and host entry: the attachment glycoprotein G and the fusion glycoprotein F. The G glycoprotein is an extensively glycosylated type II membrane protein ranging from 292 to 319 amino acids in length, existing in both a full-length transmembrane-bound form and a truncated, soluble secretory isoform lacking the initial 65 to 74 amino acids. Its structural ectodomain contains variable mucin-like regions, a heparin-binding site, and a highly conserved central domain possessing a specialized CX3C chemokine motif. This motif functionally mimics the human chemokine fractalkine, competing directly for binding to CX3CR1 receptors located on airway epithelial cells, T lymphocytes, and natural killer cells, thereby suppressing directional immune cell chemotaxis and dampening type I interferon secretion. Epitopic divergence across the G protein formally classifies the virus into two distinct antigenic lineages, designated group A and group B. Concurrently, the fusion glycoprotein F constitutes a genetically stable trimer of 574 amino acids that undergoes dramatic conformational remodeling from a metastable prefusion state to an energetically stable postfusion conformation. Binding cellular targets such as nucleolin via its ribonucleic acid-binding domains, alongside auxiliary receptors like toll-like receptor 4, epidermal growth factor receptor, and intercellular adhesion molecule 1, the F protein drives the hallmark formation of multinucleated syncytia through cell-to-cell membrane fusion, with the prefusion theta antigenic segment eliciting the highest concentrations of potent neutralizing antibodies. Discover more Engineering Job Board Research Paper Access Learn Engineering Track Biotech Tour Colleges Complementing these primary surface antigens are internal structural, regulatory, and non-structural proteins that govern viral lifecycle dynamics and suppress host defenses. The small hydrophobic protein forms a pentameric transmembrane ion channel within the viral envelope that enhances virulence while actively attenuating tumor necrosis factor alpha signaling pathways. Within the virion core, the viral genome is encased by more than two thousand copies of the conserved nucleoprotein N, each protecting seven ribonucleic acid nucleotides and presenting a primary immunogenic target for host cytotoxic CD8-positive T lymphocytes. The viral phosphoprotein acts as an indispensable catalytic cofactor that bridges the large polymerase protein L to synthesize active ribonucleoprotein complexes, driving replication through an inherently error-prone enzymatic mechanism devoid of proofreading capabilities, which fosters single-nucleotide polymorphisms that facilitate therapeutic evasion. Virion architecture is stabilized by the phosphorylated, non-glycosylated matrix protein M, which physically bridges the helical nucleocapsid to the viral envelope by engaging the cytoplasmic tail of the fusion glycoprotein. Crucially, the non-structural proteins NS1 and NS2, expressed exclusively inside infected host cells and localizing to mitochondria, actively degrade antiviral signaling by disrupting dendritic cell maturation, suppressing type I interferon production, and blocking regulatory ubiquitination cascades on RIG-I and MDA5 sensors. The transmission dynamics and seasonal distribution of the virus exhibit pronounced geographic and climatic variability, alongside substantial disruptions recorded during the coronavirus pandemic. The pathogen spreads readily through direct contact with contaminated surfaces and inhalation of nasopharyngeal droplets expelled during coughing, sneezing, talking, or breathing, with environmental viral viability lasting up to six hours on hard inanimate objects, ninety minutes on disposable gloves, and twenty minutes on human skin. Epidemiological calculations establish a baseline reproduction number ranging between 1 and 5, typically clustering near 3, indicating that an infected index patient routinely transmits the pathogen to three secondary contacts. In temperate climates of the Northern Hemisphere, epidemics reliably emerge in November, peak between January and February, and subside by March, whereas Southern Hemisphere circulation peaks predominantly from June through September. In tropical environments like Malaysia and Mozambique, viral transmission coincides with protracted rainy seasons and can persist continuously for up to ten months. European multicenter surveillance indicates a marked west-to-east continental gradient, with Eastern European transmission commencing approximately four weeks later than in Western Europe. Following the aggressive implementation of pandemic restrictions in 2020, viral detection dropped precipitously—falling from 5.4 percent to 0.03 percent in the United States and registering reductions exceeding 98 percent across Belgium and Australia—before sparking intense, unseasonal resurgences across Europe, North America, and Asia during late 2021 and 2022 due to relaxed social restrictions, diminished population herd immunity, and resumed international travel. At the microscopic tissue level, the pathogen demonstrates strong primary cellular tropism for ciliated columnar epithelial cells lining the human respiratory tract, while also infecting deeper lower airway targets including pneumocytes, basal epithelial cells, and resident dendritic cells. Initial viral replication in the nasopharyngeal epithelium is followed by rapid contiguous spread toward terminal bronchioles through direct intercellular extension and mobile infected macrophages. Viral propagation induces widespread cellular necrosis, sloughing, and profound ciliary disruption. The subsequent host immune response triggers intense local infiltration dominated by neutrophils, cytotoxic T lymphocytes, and recruited monocytes, accompanied by an outpouring of proinflammatory cytokines including tumor necrosis factor alpha, interleukin-1, and interleukin-6 that activate nuclear factor kappa B signaling. In epithelial tissues, enhanced interleukin-8 secretion recruits vast swathes of neutrophils, whose degranulation, phagocytosis, and release of extracellular traps exacerbate acute local tissue damage. Concurrently, activated eosinophils release chemical mediators linked to acute wheezing and secondary bronchial reactivity. The resulting mucosal edema, accelerated plasma extravasation, sloughed necrotic debris, and abundant hypersecreted mucus cause widespread intraluminal plugging throughout the narrow pediatric bronchial tree. This mechanical obstruction leads to focal air trapping and secondary resorption atelectasis, driving severe clinical manifestations including wheezing, cyanosis, tachypnea, and respiratory failure, while predisposing affected infants to persistent long-term airway sequelae such as recurring childhood wheezing, allergic sensitization, and adult asthma. Accurate clinical differentiation of the virus requires precise laboratory diagnostic modalities because presenting symptoms overlap indistinguishably with influenza, parainfluenza, and other seasonal respiratory viruses. Nasopharyngeal aspirates and modern flocked swabs, which harvest high densities of intact epithelial cells, represent the gold-standard specimen collection methods, yielding substantially higher viral titers in young pediatric patients than in geriatric cohorts. Historically, viral cell culture utilizing permissive cell lines—including Hep-2, A549, and rhesus monkey kidney cells—served as the definitive reference standard, identifying viral growth through visible cytopathic effects like giant multinucleated syncytia and cellular lysis over a two- to five-day incubation period. However, conventional culture has been largely supplanted in routine diagnostics by real-time reverse transcription–polymerase chain reaction assays targeting highly conserved sequences within the viral N, F, G, and L genes. Quantitative molecular testing offers exceptional clinical sensitivity and specificity, providing cycle threshold metrics that correlate with patient viral loads and therapeutic response, although residual shed viral fragments can linger weeks following clinical resolution. Point-of-care rapid molecular platforms authorized by international regulatory bodies—including Alere, cobas Liat, and Cepheid Xpert systems—deliver rapid results that streamline acute triage. In parallel, rapid antigen detection tests and automated optical immunoassays, such as BD Veritor and Quidel Sofia platforms, provide rapid bedside results, although their diagnostic sensitivity fluctuates significantly depending on patient age, symptom onset, and underlying viral load. Modern prophylactic immunization against the pathogen represents a historic clinical triumph achieved decades after catastrophic initial trials. In the 1960s, a formalin-inactivated whole-virus vaccine candidate administered to infants inadvertently induced vaccine-associated enhanced respiratory disease upon subsequent natural exposure, leading to the tragic deaths of two vaccinated children. Mechanistic investigations later demonstrated that this enhanced pathology stemmed from non-neutralizing, low-avidity antibody generation coupled with an unbalanced, hyperinflammatory Th2-biased cellular immune response. Decades of structural biology research ultimately resolved the atomic structure of the prefusion conformation of the fusion glycoprotein, unlocking modern antigen stabilization. By mid-2023, the United States Food and Drug Administration approved Arexvy, an AS01E-adjuvanted recombinant prefusion F subunit vaccine developed by GSK, indicated for adults aged sixty and older. This was promptly followed by the regulatory approval of Pfizer’s bivalent recombinant prefusion F vaccine Abrysvo, indicated for older adults and uniquely authorized for maternal administration between gestational weeks 32 and 36 to confer passive transplacental protective antibodies to newborns through their initial six months of life. In 2024, Moderna’s single-dose messenger ribonucleic acid vaccine mRESVIA gained approval for adults aged sixty and above, delivering encapsulated genetic transcripts encoding the membrane-anchored prefusion F glycoprotein. Meta-analytic evaluations reveal that Arexvy reduces related hospitalizations in older adults by 60 to 65 percent, Abrysvo achieves 58 to 63 percent efficacy in older adults and 68 to 72 percent protection in infants via maternal immunization, and mRESVIA provides 55 to 58 percent overall clinical protection, with all three platforms driving durable five- to seven-fold surges in neutralizing antibody titers lasting at least twelve months. Complementing active immunization strategies are specialized passive monoclonal antibodies and targeted pharmaceutical therapies developed for high-risk patients. While routine medical management centers primarily on supportive interventions—including supplemental oxygen therapy, gentle hydration, saline nasal drops, and mechanical ventilation during respiratory failure—the broad-spectrum guanosine analog ribavirin remains officially approved as an aerosolized antiviral formulation for severe pediatric lower respiratory tract infections. However, aerosolized ribavirin is rarely deployed in routine practice due to significant financial costs, complex administration protocols, potential teratogenicity, and conflicting efficacy data. Systematic meta-analyses across hospitalized cohorts demonstrate no statistically significant reduction in overall mortality among patients receiving ribavirin versus supportive care, exhibiting a risk ratio of 0.63, although profound mortality reductions emerge in specific subgroups such as patients with underlying hematological malignancies (risk ratio 0.32). Among targeted biologics, palivizumab, an established recombinant human monoclonal antibody directed against the F glycoprotein, has provided monthly passive protection for premature and medically fragile infants for over two decades. More recently, nirsevimab, an engineered long-acting recombinant human IgG1 monoclonal antibody possessing an extended serum half-life, received formal approval and broad regulatory endorsement, providing comprehensive, single-dose seasonal protection against severe lower tract disease for all infants younger than eight months entering their first viral season, while investigational candidates continue advancing to circumvent historical resistance mutations that halted previous drugs like suptavumab. Beyond its traditional role as an aggressive respiratory pathogen, emerging biomedical research investigates the novel application of the virus as a targeted oncolytic agent. Naturally occurring and genetically engineered oncolytic viruses are specifically harnessed to selectively invade, replicate inside, and destroy malignant cells while leaving healthy host tissues undamaged. Preclinical evaluations utilizing the RSV-A2 strain demonstrate pronounced oncolytic activity across multiple malignant cell models, including PC-3 and LNCaP prostate carcinoma lines, A431 skin cancer lines, Hep3B and Huh-7 hepatocellular carcinoma cells, and TC-1 models of human papillomavirus-related cervical cancer. Moreover, the inherent biological capacity of the viral F glycoprotein to mediate syncytia formation substantially enhances its antineoplastic utility, facilitating lateral spread across the dense tumor microenvironment and killing uninfected adjacent malignant cells through widespread multinucleated fusion. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

RSV 疫苗 建议成人使用,因为 65 岁及以上的人占住院治疗的 49.2%

RSV Vaccine Recommended for Adults as People 65 and Older Account for 49.2% of Hospitalizations
新闻简报该文章强调,人们越来越认识到 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 是成年人(尤其是 65 岁及以上老年人)的一个重大健康问题。根据韩国疾病管理和预防机构的数据,截至 2026 年 10 月 3 日,这些人占 RSV 住院病例的 49.2%,超过了婴幼儿的 36.6%。RSV 可引起严重的下呼吸道疾病,特别是对于患有心脏病或肺病、糖尿病或免疫系统减弱等潜在疾病的成年人。文章指出,目前韩国批准了三种成人用 RSV 疫苗,其中包括 2026 年 8 月批准的 Abrysvo。临床试验表明,Abrysvo 第一季可将 RSV 下呼吸道疾病风险降低 88.9%,两季合并时可降低 81.5%。但RSV 疫苗不属于国家免疫规划的覆盖范围,个人必须付费。文章还提到孕妇可以接受 Abrysvo,因为抗体通过胎盘转移给胎儿,可能为婴儿出生后的前六个月提供保护。尽管有这些进展,目前 RSV 暴发 的确切原因仍不清楚,分析师认为人口老龄化和扩大检测等因素可能是造成这种情况的原因。
查看新闻五要素
时间
文章发表于2026年11月24日。RSV 感染通常在 10 月开始上升,并持续到次年 3 月。引用的住院数据截至 2026 年 10 月 3 日。
地点与对象
该报告重点关注韩国,RSV 被列为国家指定传染病 4 类。受影响人群包括65岁及以上的成年人,占RSV住院人数的49.2%,以及0至6岁的婴幼儿,占36.6%。
事件
文章报道,韩国疾病管理和预防机构建议成年人,特别是 65 岁及以上的老年人使用 RSV 疫苗接种,因为他们患严重疾病的风险很高。它还强调,RSV 可导致成人严重下呼吸道疾病,尤其是患有基础疾病的成人。
规模、影响与风险
截至 2026 年 10 月 3 日,在 6,706 名住院 RSV 患者中,49.2% 年龄在 65 岁及以上,超过婴幼儿的 36.6%。文章指出,RSV 可导致成人肺炎或呼吸困难,高危人群,包括患有慢性病或免疫系统较弱的人群,应考虑 疫苗接种。
应对、状态与不确定性
用于成人的RSV 疫苗目前在韩国获得批准,其中包括于2026年8月获得批准的Abrysvo。Abrysvo的预防功效在临床试验中得到证实,第一季可将RSV下呼吸道疾病的风险降低88.9%,两季合并时可降低81.5%。但国家免疫规划不包括RSV 疫苗,个人必须自行付费。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

RSV Vaccine Recommended for Adults as People 65 and Older Account for 49.2% of Hospitalizations

News Brief
The article highlights the increasing recognition of respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) as a significant health concern for adults, particularly those aged 65 and older. According to data from the Korea Disease Control and Prevention Agency, these individuals accounted for 49.2% of RSV hospitalizations through October 3, 2026, surpassing the 36.6% seen in infants and young children. RSV can cause severe lower respiratory tract disease, especially in adults with underlying conditions such as heart or lung disease, diabetes, or weakened immune systems. The article notes that three RSV vaccines for adults are currently approved in South Korea, including Abrysvo, which was approved in August 2026. Clinical trials showed that Abrysvo reduced the risk of RSV lower respiratory tract disease by 88.9% in the first season and 81.5% when combining two seasons. However, RSV vaccines are not covered by the national immunization program, and individuals must pay for them. The article also mentions that pregnant women can receive Abrysvo, as antibodies are transferred to the fetus through the placenta, potentially offering protection to infants during their first six months of life. Despite these developments, the exact cause of the current RSV outbreak remains unclear, with analysts suggesting factors such as population aging and expanded testing may be contributing.
Original source text (Korean)
RSV Vaccine Recommended for Adults as People 65 and Older Account for 49.2% of Hospitalizations by Koo Kyo Yun Updated 2026/11/24 08:00 Abrysvo / Photo courtesy of Korea Pfizer Pharmaceuticals As cold winds arrive, more people visit hospitals with runny noses, coughs and chills. Most people first think of a cold or influenza, but another virus can cause similar symptoms. It is respiratory syncytial virus, or RSV. RSV infection is an acute respiratory infection caused by respiratory syncytial virus, and in South Korea it is managed as a Class 4 nationally designated infectious disease. Two types, A and B, circulate at the same time. Patient numbers usually begin to rise in October and continue through March of the following year. The virus spreads through contact with an infected person's nasal discharge or phlegm, or through droplets sprayed by coughing or sneezing. After an incubation period of 2 to 8 days, symptoms similar to those of a cold, including a runny nose, nasal congestion, cough and fever, appear. Healthy adults generally recover within one to two weeks, but if the virus invades the bronchi and lungs, it can lead to pneumonia or breathing difficulties. ���Adults also get RSV, with older adults and people with underlying conditions at higher risk of severe disease A common misconception about RSV is that it is a virus dangerous only to infants and young children. This is because it is known to be a major cause of bronchiolitis and pneumonia in infants and young children. RSV does not discriminate by age. Even if a person had the infection as a child, immunity does not last a lifetime, so adults can become infected again. Hospitalization statistics also demonstrate this. According to infectious disease sentinel surveillance data from the Korea Disease Control and Prevention Agency, among the 6,706 RSV patients hospitalized through October 3 this year, people 65 and older accounted for 49.2%, or 3,299 patients, exceeding the 36.6%, or 2,455 patients, among infants and young children ages 0 to 6. The exact cause has not been identified, but analysts say the introduction of preventive antibody injections for infants, population aging and expanded testing may have contributed. As people age, immune function declines, and symptoms can become more severe from the same infection. Symptoms that begin like a cold can develop into pneumonia, or worsen preexisting conditions and lead to hospitalization or intensive care. Age is not the only factor that increases risk. People with longstanding heart or lung disease, some people with diabetes and those with weakened immune systems are also at higher risk of severe disease. This is why the U.S. Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, CDC, advises considering age along with underlying conditions and other health factors when preventing RSV in adults. Professor Lee Rae-seok of the Division of Infectious Diseases at Seoul St. Mary's Hospital said, "Both RSV subtypes, A and B, are circulating, and because the virus can lead to severe lower respiratory tract disease in older adults and people with underlying conditions, prevention is important." ���Three adult RSV vaccines approved, opening vaccination to pregnant women There is generally no specific antiviral treatment used for RSV. Treatment focuses on easing symptoms through fluid intake, rest and fever reducers, while severe cases may require oxygen therapy or hospitalization. For this reason, high-risk groups should prepare in advance through vaccination. Three RSV vaccines for adults are currently approved in South Korea. Following Korea GSK's Arexvy in December 2024 and Moderna Korea's mRESVIA in December 2025, Korea Pfizer Pharmaceuticals' Abrysvo received approval from the Ministry of Food and Drug Safety last August. Abrysvo, the most recently approved vaccine, is a bivalent vaccine containing antigens for both RSV A and B. It reflects the characteristics of RSV, in which the two types circulate together. The preventive efficacy of Abrysvo was confirmed in a global Phase 3 clinical trial involving about 37,000 people aged 60 and older. During the first RSV season, it reduced the risk of RSV lower respiratory tract disease accompanied by at least three symptoms by 88.9%, and the figure was 81.5% when the two seasons were analyzed together. Similar results were found in clinical practice. Kaiser Permanente Southern California in the United States analyzed data from three RSV seasons involving adults aged 60 and older and found that Abrysvo reduced hospitalizations or emergency department visits due to RSV lower respiratory tract disease by 73%. More than 90% of those included in the analysis had underlying conditions such as asthma, chronic obstructive pulmonary disease, heart failure or diabetes, and about 20% were immunocompromised. One difference between Abrysvo and existing adult vaccines is that pregnant women can also receive it. When administered at 28 to 36 weeks of pregnancy, antibodies produced in the mother's body are transferred to the fetus through the placenta, creating the expectation that they can prevent RSV lower respiratory tract disease in the baby through the first six months after birth. Until now, infant RSV prevention has primarily involved directly injecting antibodies into babies after birth, but Abrysvo differs in that it provides protection before birth. Professor Lee Rae-seok said, "Abrysvo can also be used to prevent RSV lower respiratory tract disease in adults aged 60 and older and high-risk adults ages 18 to 59," adding, "Its distinctive feature is that vaccination during pregnancy can protect infants from immediately after birth through the first six months of life." However, RSV vaccines are not yet covered by the national immunization program, so individuals must pay the cost themselves. It is advisable to decide whether to receive the vaccine after consulting a medical professional and considering age, underlying conditions and usual health status. Experts recommend that people with weakened immune systems, chronic conditions such as heart or lung disease and older adults check whether vaccination is necessary before the RSV season. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

瑞士模型证明母体 RSV 疫苗接种 对婴儿感染具有成本效益 - Bioengineer.org

Maternal RSV Vaccination Proves Cost-Effective Against Infant Infections in Swiss Model - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报A study conducted by the Swiss Federal Office of Public Health, commissioned in 2025, evaluated the cost-effectiveness of maternal RSVpreF vaccination against infant RSV infections. The research, published on November 24, 2026, compared maternal immunization with direct infant immunization using nirsevimab and no prophylaxis. The model, developed for a cohort of 38,844 infants born between October and March, demonstrated that maternal vaccination significantly reduced healthcare costs by nearly 9.878 million Swiss francs while slightly decreasing quality-adjusted life years. RSV is a major seasonal pathogen in Switzerland, with peak activity in January, and nearly 90% of children are infected before age two. The study highlights the burden of RSV on the healthcare system, including a threefold increase in hospital admissions and high medical expenses. The model used a Markov cohort framework, which simplified the analysis by excluding complex transmission assumptions. However, it acknowledged limitations such as the omission of herd immunity and the lack of Swiss outpatient infection data. The findings support the use of maternal vaccination as a cost-effective strategy for RSV prevention in infants, aligning with the regulatory approval of the RSVpreF vaccine in August 2024.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究从瑞士医疗保健支付者的角度对每年 10 月至 3 月出生的婴儿进行了 队列研究 调查,该模型模拟了一年时间范围内的一个月周期。发布日期为2026年11月24日。
地点与对象
该研究重点关注瑞士的婴儿,特别是 10 月至 3 月之间出生的婴儿,队列研究 总共有 38,844 名婴儿。该模型还考虑了足月儿和晚期早产儿的孕龄分层。
事件
为瑞士联邦公共卫生办公室开发的综合卫生技术评估模型表明,与使用 尼塞韦单抗 且不进行预防的婴儿直接免疫相比,使用 RSVpreF 疫苗 进行 呼吸道合胞病毒 孕产妇免疫是一种具有成本效益的公共卫生策略。该研究由瑞士联邦公共卫生办公室于 2025 年委托进行。
规模、影响与风险
经济评估表明,孕产妇 疫苗接种 极大地改变了整个医疗保健系统的临床和财务结果,总体成本减少了 987.8738 瑞士法郎,同时质量调整生命年减少了十八点四。RSV 是瑞士的主要季节性病原体,传播 从 11 月到 4 月流行,并在 1 月达到高峰。
应对、状态与不确定性
瑞士联邦公共卫生办公室委托约克健康经济联盟执行正式的卫生技术评估。该模型使用马尔可夫 队列研究 框架,由于其在表示 疫苗接种 结果方面的简单性和准确性,因此选择了动态 传播 模型。该研究承认方法学上的局限性,包括忽略群体免疫的静态框架、排除孕产妇临床获益、缺乏瑞士门诊感染登记,以及分析范围仅限于十二个月。

Maternal RSV Vaccination Proves Cost-Effective Against Infant Infections in Swiss Model - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A study conducted by the Swiss Federal Office of Public Health, commissioned in 2025, evaluated the cost-effectiveness of maternal RSVpreF vaccination against infant RSV infections. The research, published on November 24, 2026, compared maternal immunization with direct infant immunization using nirsevimab and no prophylaxis. The model, developed for a cohort of 38,844 infants born between October and March, demonstrated that maternal vaccination significantly reduced healthcare costs by nearly 9.878 million Swiss francs while slightly decreasing quality-adjusted life years. RSV is a major seasonal pathogen in Switzerland, with peak activity in January, and nearly 90% of children are infected before age two. The study highlights the burden of RSV on the healthcare system, including a threefold increase in hospital admissions and high medical expenses. The model used a Markov cohort framework, which simplified the analysis by excluding complex transmission assumptions. However, it acknowledged limitations such as the omission of herd immunity and the lack of Swiss outpatient infection data. The findings support the use of maternal vaccination as a cost-effective strategy for RSV prevention in infants, aligning with the regulatory approval of the RSVpreF vaccine in August 2024.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Wednesday, November 25, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Maternal RSV Vaccination Proves Cost-Effective Against Infant Infections in Swiss Model by November 24, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Maternal RSV Vaccination Proves Cost-Effective Against Infant Infections in Swiss Model Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A comprehensive health technology assessment model developed for the Swiss Federal Office of Public Health indicates that maternal immunisation against respiratory syncytial virus using the RSVpreF vaccine represents a cost-effective public health strategy when compared both with direct infant immunisation using nirsevimab and with no prophylaxis. The economic evaluation, conducted from the perspective of the Swiss healthcare payer across an annual cohort of thirty-eight thousand eight hundred and forty-four infants born between October and March, demonstrated that maternal vaccination substantiality alters clinical and financial outcomes across the healthcare system. Against newborn nirsevimab prophylaxis, maternal vaccination generated an overall cost reduction of nine million eight hundred seventy-eight thousand seven hundred and thirty-eight Swiss francs while yielding eighteen point four fewer quality-adjusted life years. Respiratory syncytial virus represents a major seasonal pathogen that triggers annual epidemics across Switzerland, with transmissions characteristically running from November through April and reaching peak activity during January. While common presentations involve upper respiratory symptoms such as cough, sore throat, sneezing, and runny nose, severe infections frequently progress to lower respiratory tract illnesses including bronchiolitis, bronchitis, and pneumonia. Epidemiological records demonstrate that respiratory syncytial virus infects nearly ninety percent of children before they attain two years of age, with lower respiratory tract complications manifesting in fifteen to fifty percent of primary infections. In the long term, infant respiratory syncytial virus illness is associated with an elevated likelihood of developing asthma, exacerbating existing asthma symptoms, or suffering recurrent wheezing. Swiss administrative records indicated that inpatient hospital admissions bearing a primary diagnosis of the virus rose nearly threefold from one thousand and twenty-four to two thousand eight hundred and forty-seven. In Switzerland, inpatient hospitalisations for respiratory syncytial virus carry substantial clinical burdens and financial expenditures, with historical mean inpatient medical expenses reaching eight thousand and forty-six euros per admission, two-thirds of which involve infants. Surveillance data further reveal that more than ninety percent of infants under twelve months admitted with this infection lacked pre-existing clinical risk profiles such as premature birth, bronchopulmonary dysplasia, or congenital heart defects. Furthermore, approximately one to two percent of the total Swiss annual birth cohort requires admission to intermediate care or intensive care units because of severe viral complications. Prior to September twenty twenty-four, standard Swiss clinical care provided no routine prophylaxis for newborns and infants. Prophylaxis practices changed when nirsevimab, a long-acting monoclonal antibody, was incorporated into mandatory health insurance reimbursement as standard infant care, following the August twenty twenty-four regulatory approval by Swissmedic of the maternal RSVpreF vaccine. To assist regulatory and reimbursement determinations, the Swiss Federal Office of Public Health commissioned the York Health Economics Consortium in twenty twenty-five to execute a formal health technology assessment. Researchers constructed an Excel-based Markov cohort model to evaluate maternal RSVpreF administered between thirty-two and thirty-six weeks of gestation against newborn nirsevimab prophylaxis or no preventive prophylaxis. Investigators selected a Markov cohort framework over a dynamic transmission model because it accurately represented essential vaccination outcomes without requiring unverified transmission assumptions or excessive mathematical complexity. The model simulated the cohort using one-month cycles over a one-year time horizon, ensuring every infant experienced a complete seasonal epidemic period regardless of birth month. Newborns born at less than thirty-two weeks of gestational age were excluded because the regulatory indication for maternal RSVpreF is restricted to pregnancies between thirty-two and thirty-six weeks. The simulated cohort was stratified according to gestational age into full-term infants born at thirty-seven weeks or later and late preterm infants delivered between thirty-two and thirty-six weeks. This stratification permitted researchers to capture distinct clinical patterns across term categories, including differential vaccine efficacy, background infant mortality rates, general ward inpatient mortality, and the proportion of hospital admissions requiring intensive care unit admission. Newborns entered the Markov model in an uninfected health state immediately following birth. Each monthly cycle, individuals could remain uninfected, transition to an acute infection health state requiring medical attention, or die from background or disease-related causes. Infection states were explicitly defined by the care setting demanded by health-seeking behaviour, separating individuals into outpatient primary care visits, emergency department attendances, general hospital ward admissions, or intensive care unit hospitalisations. Because mild or asymptomatic viral cases exert negligible influence on clinical resource utilisation and quality of life decrements, the model accounted exclusively for infections prompting healthcare-seeking encounters. After spending one monthly cycle in an active infection state, surviving infants transitioned into a post-infection health state where they remained until the conclusion of the one-year analytical horizon. Owing to a lack of robust published data regarding recurring infections within a single year, the model assumed infants experienced at most one infection during the analytical timeframe. Transition probabilities under the comparator arm receiving no prophylaxis reflected baseline epidemiological incidence. For baseline hospitalisation rates, investigators used Swiss observational data, whereas emergency department and primary care attendances were informed by published Spanish observational datasets because domestic outpatient surveillance statistics were unavailable. All baseline rates were adjusted by calendar month to replicate seasonal epidemiological fluctuations. Clinical efficacy parameters governing preventive therapies were derived from international multicentre trials. Protection afforded by maternal RSVpreF was informed by the double-blind phase three MATISSE trial, with reported efficacy against severe medically attended lower respiratory illness serving as a proxy for hospital admission prevention, while efficacy against all medically attended lower respiratory illnesses informed outpatient endpoints. Because the trial was not powered for late preterm infants, maternal vaccine efficacy in this group was set at eighty-three point three percent of full-term efficacy based on transplacental antibody transfer studies. For newborn nirsevimab prophylaxis, efficacy against emergency department and primary care visits was obtained from the MELODY trial, which observed a hazard ratio of zero point twenty-three, while efficacy against hospitalisation was derived from the HARMONIE trial, which demonstrated eighty-nine point six percent efficacy. Both interventions incorporated linear waning of efficacy toward zero percent by age nine to ten months. Intervention coverage and healthcare resource valuations reflected specific Swiss clinical practice settings. Uptake for maternal RSVpreF was estimated at sixty-eight percent, representing the midpoint between observed Swiss maternal coverage rates for pertussis at eighty-six point two percent and influenza at forty-nine point eight percent. Nirsevimab coverage was modelled at eighty-four point six percent based on domestic neonatal vitamin K prophylaxis administration benchmarks. RSVpreF acquisition cost was fixed at two hundred and seven point fifty-three Swiss francs without an administration fee, as vaccination occurs during routine prenatal visits. Nirsevimab incurred a product acquisition price of three hundred ninety-five point sixty Swiss francs alongside an administration charge of one hundred and fifty Swiss francs. Direct medical expenses were expressed in twenty twenty-four Swiss francs, discounting future monetary costs and health effects at three percent per annum, while indirect productivity losses and patient travel costs were excluded. Comparative economic analyses demonstrated distinct clinical and financial trade-offs between maternal vaccination and alternative prophylactic strategies. When evaluated against no prophylaxis, maternal RSVpreF reduced the total volume of healthcare-attended viral infections from nine thousand and eighty-seven cases to six thousand eight hundred and ninety-six cases within the cohort. Although initial vaccination expenses elevated total cohort expenditure by four hundred seventy thousand four hundred and forty-one Swiss francs, the health gains amounted to twenty-seven point one additional quality-adjusted life years, yielding an incremental cost-effectiveness ratio of seventeen thousand three hundred and seventy-seven Swiss francs per quality-adjusted life year gained. Compared with newborn nirsevimab, maternal RSVpreF resulted in higher infection counts, increasing attended infections from five thousand one hundred and seventy cases to six thousand nine hundred and six cases due to lower relative efficacy and lower projected maternal uptake. Despite yielding lower aggregate clinical protection than nirsevimab, maternal RSVpreF achieved vast cost savings across the Swiss healthcare system by eliminating administration fees and reducing unit procurement expenses. In comparison with nirsevimab, the maternal programme generated total net budgetary savings of nearly ten million Swiss francs. In the cost-effectiveness plane, this relationship positioned maternal RSVpreF within the south-west quadrant, where an intervention delivers substantial monetary savings alongside minor decrements in overall health benefits. In this quadrant, higher incremental cost-effectiveness ratios indicate greater economic efficiency, confirming RSVpreF remains cost-effective whenever the acceptable societal willingness to pay per quality-adjusted life year falls below five hundred thirty-six thousand and eight Swiss francs. In probabilistic sensitivity simulations comprising one thousand iterations, maternal vaccination was cost-effective versus nirsevimab in more than ninety-nine point nine percent of model iterations across all tested threshold benchmarks. Extensive deterministic and probabilistic sensitivity evaluations demonstrated the structural robustness of the health technology assessment model. Univariate sensitivity testing established that varying individual parameters within plausible ranges never altered the fundamental conclusion that maternal RSVpreF reduces total spending compared with nirsevimab. Furthermore, RSVpreF became dominant across six distinct pricing reduction scenarios. While the model accounted for extensive clinical variables, authors acknowledged methodological limitations, including a static framework that omitted herd immunity, exclusion of maternal clinical benefits, lack of Swiss outpatient infection registries, and the restriction of analytical horizons to twelve months. These findings directly guided Swiss healthcare authorities, supporting reimbursement of maternal RSVpreF under mandatory health insurance.
