B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

积极预防怀孕儿童感染 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV)。- 越南.vn

Proactive prevention of respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) in children from pregnancy. - Vietnam.vn
新闻简报Vietnam.vn 于 2026 年 11 月 25 日发表的文章强调了儿童 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 的积极预防策略,特别是 3 个月以下的儿童,他们住院和严重并发症的风险很高。RSV 是幼儿下呼吸道感染的主要原因,该年龄组中 42.7% 的 毛细支气管炎 病例。专家建议孕妇在产前护理期间,特别是在旺季期间,与医生讨论RSV预防问题。越南卫生部门已将 RSV 预防纳入专业指南,包括卫生部第 1139/QD-BYT 号决定和越南妇产科医生协会 (VAGO) 指南。产科医生在怀孕期间提供咨询,而儿科医生则在产后进行随访。目前,RSV尚无专门的治疗,干预措施侧重于症状控制和呼吸支持。严重的病例可能需要入住ICU、机械通气,甚至ECMO。早年经历过严重 RSV 感染的儿童日后出现反复喘息和哮喘的风险明显更高。该文章强调需要建立产科-儿科协作模式,以确保从生命最初几个月起持续保护儿童。
查看新闻五要素
时间
文章发表于2026年11月25日。活动日期没有明确说明,但北方 RSV 的高峰期是 10 月至 3 月,南方是 5 月至 11 月。
地点与对象
本文讨论了越南的 RSV,特别是胡志明市和国家儿童医院的 RSV。它主要针对 5 岁以下的儿童,特别是 3 个月以下的儿童,他们住院和严重并发症的风险较高。
事件
该文章报告了儿童 RSV 的主动预防策略,强调了通过产科和儿科咨询预防怀孕期间 RSV 的重要性。这是一篇基于专家建议和专业指南的指导文章。
规模、影响与风险
RSV 是 3 个月以下婴儿住院的主要原因,占该组 毛细支气管炎 病例的 42.7%。严重 RSV 病例可能需要入住 ICU、有创机械通气或 ECMO。严重 RSV 感染的儿童在以后的生活中,反复喘息的风险要高出 4.3 倍,患哮喘的风险要高出 2 至 12 倍。
应对、状态与不确定性
越南卫生部门已将 RSV 预防纳入专业指南,包括 2026 年 4 月 23 日第 1139/QD-BYT 号决定以及越南妇产科医生协会 (VAGO) 的指南。产科医生在怀孕期间提供咨询,儿科医生在产后进行随访。RSV 没有特定的 治疗,干预措施侧重于症状控制和呼吸支持。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Proactive prevention of respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) in children from pregnancy. - Vietnam.vn

News Brief
The article published on November 25, 2026, by Vietnam.vn highlights proactive prevention strategies for respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) in children, particularly those under 3 months old, who are at high risk of hospitalization and severe complications. RSV is a leading cause of lower respiratory tract infections in young children, with 42.7% of bronchiolitis cases in this age group. Experts recommend that pregnant women discuss RSV prevention with their doctors during prenatal care, especially during peak seasons. The Vietnamese health sector has integrated RSV prevention into professional guidelines, including Decision No. 1139/QD-BYT from the Ministry of Health and guidelines from the Vietnam Association of Obstetricians and Gynecologists (VAGO). Obstetricians provide counseling during pregnancy, while pediatricians follow up after birth. Currently, there is no specific treatment for RSV, and interventions focus on symptom control and respiratory support. Severe cases may require ICU admission, mechanical ventilation, or even ECMO. Children who experience severe RSV infections in early life face a significantly higher risk of recurrent wheezing and asthma development later. The article emphasizes the need for a collaborative obstetrics-pediatrics model to ensure continuous protection for children from the first months of life.
Original source text (English)
English Login Homepage Topic News Political System Destination Event Travel Happy Vietnam Enterprise Product Heritage Museum Figure Multimedia Data Home Culture – Society Y tế Proactive prevention of respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) in children from pregnancy. Respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) is the leading cause of hospitalization in infants under 3 months old. To protect their delicate respiratory systems, experts recommend that pregnant women learn about and prevent the disease during pregnancy through obstetric and pediatric counseling. Đài Phát thanh và Truyền hình Thành phố Hồ Chí Minh•25/11/2026 Follow Vietnam.vn on Google Add Vietnam.vn on Google 0 RSV is one of the leading causes of lower respiratory tract infections in young children. According to a meta-analysis of 61 studies in 19 countries, RSV-related hospitalization rates are highest in infants aged 0 to under 3 months, and then decrease with age. Khám phá thêm Đọc Mẹo Du Lịch Khám phá ĐNA Đọc Tin Tức In Vietnam, this trend is also clearly evident in treatment practices. Data from the National Children's Hospital shows that among children hospitalized for bronchiolitis caused by RSV, the group under 3 months old accounts for as much as 42.7%. Tìm hiểu lịch sử At Children's Hospital 1 ( Ho Chi Minh City), it is estimated that about 25% of severe RSV cases or hospitalizations in children under 5 years old occur in the under-3-month-old group. Epidemiologically, cases in the North typically increase sharply from October to March of the following year, while in the South the peak occurs during the rainy season from May to November. Currently, there is no specific treatment for RSV infection; interventions mainly focus on symptom control and respiratory support. In severe cases, children require treatment in the intensive care unit (ICU), invasive mechanical ventilation, and may even need extracorporeal membrane oxygenation (ECMO). You may also like This burden of illness places significant financial pressure on families. A study in Ho Chi Minh City showed that the total cost per ICU admission for a child is five times higher than regular inpatient treatment, not including travel, accommodation, and the disruption of parents' income. Beyond the acute phase, children who experienced severe RSV infection in their early years face a 4.3 times higher risk of recurrent wheezing and a 2 to 12 times higher risk of developing asthma later in life. Based on practical experience, Dr. Phan Huu Phuc, Deputy Director of the National Children's Hospital and General Secretary of the Vietnam Pediatric Association, stated that the first few months of life are a period when infants are very susceptible to serious complications and have a much higher risk of needing respiratory support compared to older children. Bronchiolitis caused by RSV can progress to a severe, life-threatening lower respiratory tract infection. Therefore, in addition to closely monitoring the child's health after birth, parents should proactively discuss RSV with their doctor during pregnancy, especially when expecting to give birth during the peak season for the disease. Tìm hiểu lịch sử In response to this threat, the Vietnamese health sector has gradually incorporated RSV prevention into its professional guidelines. Specifically, Decision No. 1139/QD-BYT dated April 23, 2026, issued by the Ministry of Health , which promulgates the "National Guidelines on Reproductive Health Care Services regarding Pre-pregnancy and Prenatal Care," has integrated RSV prevention counseling. The Vietnam Association of Obstetricians and Gynecologists (VAGO) has also issued professional guidelines on RSV preF vaccination during pregnancy. This approach is consistent with recommendations from regional obstetrics, gynecology, and infectious disease associations such as Thailand, starting in 2025. Biologically, antibodies transferred from mother to child through the placenta gradually increase with gestational age and reach approximately 50% of the mother's concentration by the 32nd week. Associate Professor, Doctor Huynh Nguyen Khanh Trang, Head of the Obstetrics Department - Head of the Obstetrics Division at Hung Vuong Hospital, and Head of the Obstetrics and Gynecology Department at Pham Ngoc Thach University of Medicine, stated that pregnancy is a "window of opportunity" for mothers to prepare an immune foundation to protect their children from the very first days, when the child's immune system is still immature. Obstetricians play a crucial role in providing reliable scientific information for pregnant women to utilize at each prenatal check-up to prepare for their child's respiratory health. You may also like To ensure uninterrupted protection for children, experts emphasize the need for a collaborative obstetrics-pediatrics model. While obstetricians provide preventative counseling to pregnant women throughout their pregnancy, pediatricians follow up after birth to support families in early identification of disease signs and monitoring respiratory health. In collaboration with the Vietnamese healthcare sector in raising public awareness about this disease, Mr. Mark Kuo, General Director of Pfizer Vietnam Co., Ltd., shared that connecting knowledge between obstetrics and pediatrics helps to share and update scientific data and clinical experience on RSV in a timely manner, thereby effectively integrating preventive counseling into clinical practice to protect children from the first months of life. 住院儿童看到 呼吸道合胞病毒 转向夏季和秋季高峰 - Bioengineer.org

Hospitalized Children Saw Respiratory Syncytial Virus Shift to Summer and Autumn Peaks - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报2019 年至 2025 年在中国福建省五个地区进行的一项研究显示,随着 COVID-19 大流行期间非药物干预措施的放松,呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 的季节性高峰已从冬季转移到夏季和秋季。这项研究跟踪了 108,701 名患有急性呼吸道感染的住院儿童,发现 RSV 阳性率在严格的缓解措施期间下降,并在大流行后阶段反弹。该病毒在秋季(22.32%)和夏季(16.08%)的阳性率较高,一岁以下婴儿仍然是最容易感染的群体。该研究还注意到合并感染模式的变化,特别是人类鼻病毒的感染模式的变化,并强调需要持续的分子 监测 和针对年龄的预防策略来管理不断变化的疾病负担。作者警告说,该数据集无法直接评估 宿主 免疫力,需要进一步研究来确认观察到的变化。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究在2019年至2025年的四个大流行期间进行,在放松干预措施后观察大流行后时期,结果于2026年11月24日报告。
地点与对象
该研究主要针对中国福建省五个地区的住院儿童,整个研究期间共有 108,701 名参与者参与。
事件
据研究小组报告,一项多中心 监测 调查发现,随着 COVID-19 大流行期间放松非药物干预措施,呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 的季节性高峰从冬季转移到夏季和秋季。
规模、影响与风险
RSV阳性率在四个监测阶段中波动,政策过渡期的最低率为5.72百分比,而在疫情后阶段反弹至13.62百分比。病毒在秋季达到22.32百分比的阳性高峰,在夏季为16.08百分比的阳性率,1岁以下婴儿表现出最高的易感性,为22.30百分比。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究得出的结论是,RSV 流行病学 的变化是由于大流行缓解措施的影响。然而,作者强调,临床数据集不能直接评估 宿主 免疫力,该假设的确认需要进一步的血清学 监测 和出生 队列研究 研究。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Hospitalized Children Saw Respiratory Syncytial Virus Shift to Summer and Autumn Peaks - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A study conducted across five regions of Fujian Province, China, from 2019 to 2025 revealed that respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) has shifted its seasonal peak from winter to summer and autumn following the relaxation of non-pharmaceutical interventions during the COVID-19 pandemic. The research, which tracked 108,701 hospitalized children with acute respiratory tract infections, found that RSV positivity rates declined during strict mitigation measures and rebounded in the post-pandemic phase. The virus showed higher positivity in autumn (22.32%) and summer (16.08%), with infants under one year remaining the most vulnerable group. The study also noted changes in co-infection patterns, particularly with human rhinovirus, and highlighted the need for ongoing molecular surveillance and age-tailored prevention strategies to manage the evolving disease burden. The authors cautioned that the dataset cannot directly assess host immunity, and further research is required to confirm the observed shifts.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Wednesday, November 25, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Hospitalized Children Saw Respiratory Syncytial Virus Shift to Summer and Autumn Peaks by November 24, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 3 mins read 0 Hospitalized Children Saw Respiratory Syncytial Virus Shift to Summer and Autumn Peaks Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A multicenter surveillance investigation tracking hospitalized pediatric patients across five regions of Fujian Province, China, revealed that non-pharmaceutical interventions implemented during the COVID-19 pandemic altered the seasonal timing, age patterns, and co-detection dynamics of respiratory syncytial virus. Following the relaxation of intervention measures, the traditional winter surge of the virus did not return to its historical timing, shifting instead toward predominant transmission during the summer and autumn months. The study spanned from 2019 to 2025 across four distinct pandemic intervals: a pre-pandemic baseline designated as Phase 1, a strict non-pharmaceutical intervention period designated as Phase 2, a policy transition period designated as Phase 3, and a post-pandemic period designated as Phase 4. Across these intervals, researchers enrolled 108,701 hospitalized children presenting with acute respiratory tract infections, collecting one respiratory specimen from each child for multiplex polymerase chain reaction testing. Multiplex molecular testing demonstrated an overall respiratory syncytial virus positivity rate of 12.37 percent, identifying 13,241 positive cases among 107,015 analyzed children. Infection frequency fluctuated substantially across the four monitoring phases. From a pre-pandemic baseline positivity rate of 17.12 percent, detection declined to 9.41 percent under strict non-pharmaceutical interventions and fell to its lowest point of 5.72 percent during the policy transition period, before rebounding to 13.62 percent in the post-pandemic phase. Discover more Engineering Job Board Research Paper Access Learn Engineering Track Biotech Tour Colleges Surveillance data revealed an enduring disruption in the timing of viral circulation within this subtropical region. Rather than concentrating during the traditional winter months, post-pandemic viral activity peaked in autumn at 22.32 percent positivity and in summer at 16.08 percent positivity. According to the research team, this seasonal displacement from winter to summer and autumn has persisted across approximately three seasons without reverting to pre-pandemic seasonal patterns. Infants younger than one year of age maintained the highest vulnerability to infection throughout the observation period, recording a positivity rate of 22.30 percent. However, the demographic distribution of mixed infections changed considerably after pandemic mitigation measures were lifted. The proportion of co-infections occurring in children younger than three years dropped below 50 percent for the first time, while children aged three to six years accounted for 39.53 percent of all co-infected pediatric patients. The overall rate of respiratory syncytial virus co-detection per child tested measured 1.76 percent before the pandemic, dropped to 0.83 percent during mitigation measures, and increased to 2.12 percent in the post-pandemic period. This post-pandemic frequency marked a 2.55-fold rise over the intervention phase, with a 95 percent confidence interval spanning 2.24 to 2.90, and a 1.20-fold change compared with pre-pandemic levels, with a 95 percent confidence interval between 0.95 and 1.53. Among mixed viral profiles, co-infections involving human rhinovirus were the most prevalent combination, comprising 76.55 percent of all co-detections. Marked variations emerged among other co-circulating pathogens, particularly Mycoplasma pneumoniae, which experienced severe suppression down to 0.32 percent positivity during mitigation before rebounding to 32.61 percent in the winter. Combined detections of respiratory syncytial virus and Mycoplasma pneumoniae recovered from 1.82 percent during the mitigation period to 11.26 percent post-pandemic, whereas human rhinovirus exhibited stable detection and human adenovirus displayed an intermediate response across phases. Discover more Study Biology Study Chemistry Read Health Guides Lab Equipment Sales health The investigators noted that the expansion of co-infections into older preschool cohorts might reflect an accumulated immunity debt resulting from prolonged suppression of pathogen exposure. However, the authors explicitly highlighted that the clinical dataset cannot directly evaluate host immunity, emphasizing that confirmation of this hypothesis requires dedicated serological surveillance and birth-cohort studies. In their final assessment, the researchers concluded that COVID-19 mitigation measures altered the epidemiological dynamics of respiratory syncytial virus and co-circulating respiratory pathogens in hospitalized children. Given the persistent changes in seasonal circulation and pathogen co-infections, the authors underscored that ongoing molecular surveillance and age-tailored prevention strategies are essential to manage the re-emerging disease burden. Time
The study was conducted across four pandemic intervals from 2019 to 2025, with the post-pandemic period observed after the relaxation of intervention measures, and the findings reported on November 24, 2026.
Location and population
The research focused on hospitalized children in five regions of Fujian Province, China, with a total of 108,701 participants enrolled across the study period.
Event
A multicenter surveillance investigation found that respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) shifted its seasonal peak from winter to summer and autumn following the relaxation of non-pharmaceutical interventions during the COVID-19 pandemic, as reported by the research team.
Scale, impact and risk
RSV positivity rates fluctuated across the four monitoring phases, with the lowest rate of 5.72 percent during the policy transition period and a rebound to 13.62 percent in the post-pandemic phase. The virus peaked in autumn at 22.32 percent positivity and in summer at 16.08 percent positivity, with infants under one year showing the highest vulnerability at 22.30 percent.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study concluded that the changes in RSV epidemiology were due to the impact of pandemic mitigation measures. However, the authors emphasized that the clinical dataset cannot directly evaluate host immunity, and confirmation of the hypothesis requires further serological surveillance and birth-cohort studies.

它传染性强、专挑婴儿和老人"下手",早期症状却很像感冒。下面带你一次看懂:呼吸道合胞病毒是什么、怎么传、谁危险、如何应对与预防。 什么是呼吸道合胞病毒(RSV)? 呼吸道合胞病毒(Respiratory Syncytial Virus,简称 RSV)是一种常见的、具有高度传染性的呼吸道病毒,属于肺炎病毒科、正肺病毒属。 它的名字来源于一个特征:感染细胞后会让病变的细胞相互融合,形成"合胞体"(多个细胞核挤在一个大细胞里)。 1956 年 它首次从黑猩猩呼吸道中被分离出来,后来发现它是全球婴幼儿下呼吸道感染最重要的病原体之一。 关键: 全球范围内, 2016年 RSV感染影响了约 2480万人 ,导致 7.6万人死亡 ; 2019年 ,RSV导致约 3 300万例 呼吸道感染病例和 26 300例 5岁以下儿童死亡。 RSV 只有 1 个血清型 ,分为 A、B 两大亚型;它对热、干燥和常用消毒剂(如含氯消毒剂、酒精)较敏感, 55°C 5 分钟可灭活约 90% 。 它是怎么传播的? 飞沫、接触与污染表面是 RSV 的主要传播方式 RSV的患者和无症状感染者是主要传染源。感染者在临床症状出现前 1~2天 即有传染性,传染性通常持续 3~8天 ,但对于免疫功能低下的婴幼儿和老年人,即使临床症状消失,其传染期可长达 4周 。主要有三种传播途径: 1 飞沫传播 感染者咳嗽、打喷嚏时产生的飞沫被他人吸入,可导致感染。 2 接触传播 触摸被病毒污染的手、玩具、餐具、衣物后,再摸口鼻眼,容易"中招"。 3 气溶胶传播 密闭空间内,病毒可随气溶胶长时间悬浮,被易感者吸入。 潜伏期:呼吸道合胞病毒感染后,潜伏期通常为 2~8天 ,平均 4~6天 。 流行季节性:我国大部分地区 RSV 典型流行季为 当年 10月至次年4月 。 谁更容易"中招"且转重症? 婴幼儿和老年人是 RSV 重症的高危人群 各年龄段都可能感染 RSV,但高危人群主要集中在婴幼儿、老年人和免疫功能低下者: 👶 婴幼儿(尤其 6 个月内) 展开全文 小年龄婴儿气道窄,感染后更易发展为毛细支气管炎、肺炎等重症;早产儿、有先天性心脏病或支气管肺发育不良者风险更高。 🧓 老年人(65~70 岁以上) 老年人和患有慢性心肺疾病者感染后易出现重症,住院和死亡风险升高。 🛡 免疫功能低下者 如免疫抑制患者,感染可持续并导致严重疾病。 ⚠️ 反复感染 RSV 感染后抗体保护时间短,一个流行季内可能反复感染,且婴幼儿期重症感染与日后反复喘息、哮喘相关。 感染后有哪些表现? RSV 早期症状与普通感冒非常相似,这也是它容易被忽视的原因。根据累及上呼吸道还是下呼吸道,症状轻重差别很大: 表现类型 常见症状 提示 轻度(上呼吸道) 鼻塞、流涕、咳嗽、喉咙痛、声音嘶哑,伴或不伴发热 类似感冒,多可自愈 重度(下呼吸道) 咳嗽加重、喘息、呼吸急促、三凹征(胸骨/锁骨上/肋间隙凹陷)、鼻翼扇动、喂养困难、烦躁或嗜睡 婴儿、老人易重症 危险信号 口唇/指甲发紫(缺氧)、呼吸暂停、精神萎靡、拒食拒奶、持续高热 需立即就医 出现以下情况请紧急就医: 若出现呼吸急促、吸气时锁骨上窝、胸骨上窝、肋间隙出现明显凹陷、鼻翼扇动、嘴唇面色发紫发青,或出现喘憋、拒食、精神萎靡的症状,需立刻就医。若出现持续高热超过 3天 、频繁呕吐导致无法进食、尿量明显减少(如 6-8 小时无尿 ),或精神状态极差(嗜睡、难以唤醒),必须马上送医。有基础疾病的患儿或老年人建议尽早就医。 大多数感染者约 1~2 周内痊愈 ;再次感染通常比第一次轻,但婴幼儿期严重感染可能影响长期呼吸健康。 如何治疗?目前没有"特效药" 目前 RSV 感染 尚无特效抗病毒药物 ,治疗以对症支持为主: 支持治疗 保持呼吸道通畅、保证营养与水分、必要时吸氧;重症可能需要氧疗或管饲。 对症用药 退烧可用对乙酰氨基酚/布洛芬;鼻塞可用减充血剂;喘息可用支气管扩张剂或吸入激素缓解症状。 抗生素? RSV 是病毒, 抗生素无效 。仅在合并细菌感染(如细菌性肺炎、中耳炎)时才需用抗生素。 复方感冒药 只能缓解流涕、咳嗽等表面症状,不能杀灭病毒,婴幼儿须遵医嘱慎用。 提醒: 盲目服用抗生素不仅治不了 RSV,还会破坏肠道菌群、降低免疫力,加重身体负担。 如何预防?做好这几件事 洗手、戴口罩、勤通风——最实在的防护 预防 RSV 可分为"非药物防护"和"特异性免疫预防"两类: 一、人人都能做的非药物防护 勤洗手(饭前便后、接触婴幼儿前、外出归来,搓手 ≥20秒 );咳嗽礼仪:用纸巾/肘部遮掩口鼻;高发季少去人群密集处,必要时戴口罩每天开窗通风;不共用杯具餐具室内戒烟、避免二手烟污染物用含氯/酒精消毒剂擦拭。 二、特异性免疫预防(重点人群) 👶 婴儿 可在流行季前接种 RSV 长效单克隆抗体,通常只需注射 1 次 ,用于预防婴儿 RSV 下呼吸道感染。 🤰 孕晚期妈妈 部分国家/地区建议孕 32~36 周 接种 RSV 疫苗,通过胎盘把抗体传给宝宝,保护其出生后早期。 🧓 老年人 ≥60 岁(尤其 ≥75 岁)及有慢性病的 50~74 岁高风险人群,可咨询医生接种 RSV 疫苗。 注意: 疫苗/单抗的适用政策因地区而异,具体请以当地疾控和接种门诊的最新规定为准。 常见问答 Q:RSV 和流感、新冠是一回事吗? A:不是。它们是不同病毒,但都会引起呼吸道症状,且可同时流行。鉴别需要核酸检测等实验室手段,症状上很难单凭肉眼区分。 Q:孩子得过一次,还会再得吗? A:会。RSV 抗体保护时间短,流行季内可能反复感染,但多数再感染症状比第一次轻。 Q:普通感冒药能"治好"RSV 吗? A:不能。感冒药只缓解流涕、咳嗽等表面症状,不杀灭病毒;真正靠的是自身免疫力+对症支持。 Q:怎样判断要不要去医院? A:轻症居家观察即可;一旦出现呼吸急促、口唇发紫、呼吸暂停、精神萎靡、拒食拒奶或持续高热,尤其是婴幼儿和老人,应立即就医。 ⚠️ 声明:本文为健康科普,不能替代专业医疗诊断和治疗。如有相关症状或接种疑问,请及时就医或咨询当地疾控中心、接种门诊。 资料来源:中国疾病预防控制中心监测提示(2026)、科普中国、腾讯医典等公开科普资料。 /健康科普 来源 | 河南疾控 【免责声明:我们致力于公益宣传,无任何商业用途。我们尊重原创,也注重分享。版权原作者所有,如在使用信息时侵犯了您的利益,请及时联系,我们将在24小时之内删除。】 返回搜狐,查看更多

认识呼吸道合胞病毒(RSV),科学守护宝宝健康

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报本文提供有关 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 的一般信息,强调其在中国的季节性 患病率,特别是在 10 月至 11 月和 4 月的北方地区。RSV 是一种常见的呼吸道病原体,可导致婴儿,尤其是一岁以下婴儿,包括早产儿、低出生体重儿以及有潜在健康问题的婴儿患严重疾病。本文概述了手部卫生、通风和减少接触拥挤空间等预防策略。它还提到对高危婴儿使用 RSV 单克隆抗体,但没有报告具体的 暴发 或事件。重点是提高认识并推广积极措施,保护婴儿免受呼吸道合胞病毒感染。
查看新闻五要素
时间
本文讨论了 RSV 的季节性 患病率,指出它在秋季和冬季很常见,特别是从 10 月到 11 月,一直持续到接下来的 4 月。发布日期为2026年11月24日。
地点与对象
本文重点关注中国的RSV,特别是该病毒更为流行的北方地区。它强调了婴儿的风险,特别是一岁以下的婴儿,包括早产儿和低出生体重儿,以及有潜在健康问题的婴儿。
事件
本文提供有关 RSV 的一般信息,解释其性质、症状和 传播 方法。它不是报告特定的 暴发 或事件,而是提供有关该病毒的教育内容。
规模、影响与风险
RSV 是婴儿急性下呼吸道感染的重要原因,可能导致 毛细支气管炎 和肺炎等严重疾病。文章强调,即使是健康的婴儿也可能出现严重症状,高危人群需要特别关注。
应对、状态与不确定性
该文章概述了一般预防和应对措施,例如手部卫生、通风、减少接触以及对高危婴儿使用 RSV 单克隆抗体。它没有报告任何具体的调查或遏制工作。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
认识呼吸道合胞病毒(RSV),科学守护宝宝健康
News Brief
This article provides general information about respiratory syncytial virus (RSV), emphasizing its seasonal prevalence in China, particularly in northern regions from October to November and into April. RSV is a common respiratory pathogen that can cause severe illness in infants, especially those under one year old, including preterm and low birth weight infants, as well as those with underlying health conditions. The article outlines prevention strategies such as hand hygiene, ventilation, and reducing exposure to crowded spaces. It also mentions the use of RSV monoclonal antibodies for high-risk infants, though no specific outbreak or event is reported. The focus is on raising awareness and promoting proactive measures to protect infants from RSV infections.
Original source text (Chinese)
进入秋冬季,气温走低、昼夜温差加大,呼吸道传染病进入高发季节。在众多呼吸道病原体中,有一种病毒常披着普通感冒的 “ 外衣 ” 侵袭小宝宝,容易被家长忽视 —— 它就是呼吸道合胞病毒( Respiratory syncytial virus ,简称 RSV )。 RSV 是引起婴幼儿急性下呼吸道感染的重要病原体。 (一)认识 RSV :它是什么 RSV 是一种常见的呼吸道病毒,不是细菌,也不是新型病毒。人群普遍易感,感染后仍可再次感染。在我国北方地区, RSV 流行主要集中在每年 10—11 月至次年 4 月。 感染后的表现因人而异: 轻症:类似普通感冒,可出现鼻塞、流涕、咳嗽、低热等表现; 重症:病毒可向下侵袭气道和肺部,出现喘息、呼吸急促或费力、喂养困难、拒奶、精神萎靡等表现;小婴儿还可能以吃奶差、嗜睡为主要表现,需要特别警惕。 1 岁以内婴儿,尤其是早产儿、低出生体重儿,以及合并先天性心脏病、慢性肺病、免疫缺陷等基础疾病的宝宝,感染后更易发展为毛细支气管炎、肺炎,严重时需要住院治疗。需要提醒的是, RSV 并非只 “ 偏爱 ” 体弱宝宝,部分健康足月出生、没有基础疾病的婴儿感染后同样可能发展为重症,所有宝宝都应重视预防。 (二) RSV 怎样传播 RSV 传播能力较强,主要有以下三种途径: 飞沫传播:感染者咳嗽、打喷嚏、说话时喷出携带病毒的飞沫,近距离被他人吸入呼吸道,是最常见的传播方式。 接触传播:病毒可在玩具、门把手、餐具、衣物等物体表面存活一段时间;手接触被污染物品后再揉眼睛、摸鼻子或嘴巴,病毒可经黏膜侵入身体;亲吻、近距离抱孩子也可能造成直接接触传播。 气溶胶传播:在密闭、通风较差的空间内,病毒细小颗粒可在空气中悬浮一段时间,被他人吸入后感染,多见于室内聚集场所。 展开全文 托幼机构、家庭内容易发生交叉感染,需要格外注意。 (三)如何预防 RSV 1. 做好日常卫生与防护 勤洗手:外出回家、接触宝宝前,务必用流动水和肥皂(洗手液)认真洗手;咳嗽、打喷嚏时用肘部或纸巾遮挡口鼻。 常通风:每日定时开窗通风;定期擦拭、清洁门把手、桌面、玩具、奶瓶等宝宝高频接触的物品。 减少暴露:流行季节尽量少带小月龄宝宝前往室内游乐场、大型商超等密闭、人员密集场所;亲友患病期间避免上门探望和近距离接触孩子;外出必要时佩戴口罩。 隔离防护:家中如有成员感冒咳嗽,应佩戴口罩,减少与宝宝的近距离接触,避免亲吻宝宝。 2. 可在医生评估后使用 RSV 单克隆抗体 目前 RSV 感染尚缺乏特效抗病毒药物,发病后以对症支持治疗为主,对高危宝宝而言,避免感染尤为重要。 什么是 RSV 单克隆抗体: RSV 单克隆抗体属于被动免疫制剂,是将针对 RSV 的高效抗体直接输入体内,为宝宝提供针对性免疫保护。它与疫苗不同 —— 疫苗是刺激机体自身产生免疫力,单抗则是直接补充保护性抗体,起效较快,用于婴幼儿 RSV 暴露前预防。 适用人群:适用于即将进入或出生在第一个 RSV 流行季的新生儿和婴儿;早产儿、低出生体重儿、先天性心脏病、慢性肺病、免疫缺陷等重症高危婴儿,可由医生重点评估。 (四)出现这些情况,请及时就医 在家中一旦发现宝宝出现以下任一表现,应及时带宝宝前往医院就诊评估,不要自行囤药、盲目 “ 硬扛 ” : 呼吸急促或呼吸费力、鼻翼扇动、胸壁凹陷; 口唇或指甲发青发紫、喘息明显; 喂养困难、拒奶; 精神萎靡、异常嗜睡或烦躁不安; 尿量明显减少。 秋冬季呼吸道疾病防控,重在主动预防、科学防范。做好日常卫生、减少暴露风险,必要时在医生指导下选择针对性免疫保护,多管齐下,才能最大程度守护孩子的呼吸道健康。 返回搜狐,查看更多

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

CDC 数据显示,RSV 季节比去年晚 69 天达到顶峰,比婴儿抗体窗口期更持久 - Tech Times

RSV Season Peaked 69 Days Later Than Last Year, Outlasting Infant Antibody Window, CDC Data Show - Tech Times
新闻简报The 2025–26 US RSV season peaked 69 days later than the previous year, outlasting the infant antibody window. The peak weekly RSV hospital admission rate was 3.0 per 100,000, about 23% below the previous year. Infant hospitalization rates were lower than in the prior year, with rates for infants 0–11 months at 790.2 per 100,000 and for children 12–23 months at 611.9 per 100,000. There were 34 RSV deaths among children reported by 35 jurisdictions from September 28, 2025, to June 30, 2026, with 19 (55.9%) having an underlying condition and 16 (47.1%) hospitalized. CDC guidance suggests that infant RSV antibodies should be given October through the end of March, with some states extending the window into April. The FDA is raising safety questions about the shots, but no safety issues have been identified in clinical studies or after more than 6 million babies were immunized worldwide.
查看新闻五要素
时间
2023 年 6 月 14 日,家长们带着孩子戴着口罩步行前往圣地亚哥的 Sagrado Corazon 学校,当时智利因呼吸道病毒 暴发 严重而恢复在学校使用口罩。
地点与对象
美国,数据来自 CDC 国家实验室 监测 和基于医院的展望。
事件
根据 CDC 数据,2025-26 年美国 RSV 季节比上一年晚 69 天达到峰值,比婴儿抗体窗口持续时间更长。本赛季比去年晚 27 天结束。
规模、影响与风险
The peak weekly RSV hospital admission rate was 3.0 per 100,000 in the week ending February 21, 2026, about 23% below the previous year. Infant hospitalization rates were lower than in the prior year, with rates for infants 0–11 months at 790.2 per 100,000 and for children 12–23 months at 611.9 per 100,000. There were 34 RSV deaths among children reported by 35 jurisdictions from September 28, 2025, to June 30, 2026, with 19 (55.9%) having an underlying condition and 16 (47.1%) hospitalized.
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC guidance suggests that infant RSV antibodies should be given October through the end of March, with some states extending the window into April. The CDC report does not analyze when infants were dosed relative to their illness, and the effectiveness of the antibody shots is not estimated. The FDA is raising safety questions about the shots, but no safety issues have been identified in clinical studies or after more than 6 million babies were immunized worldwide.

RSV Season Peaked 69 Days Later Than Last Year, Outlasting Infant Antibody Window, CDC Data Show - Tech Times

News Brief
The 2025–26 US RSV season peaked 69 days later than the previous year, outlasting the infant antibody window. The peak weekly RSV hospital admission rate was 3.0 per 100,000, about 23% below the previous year. Infant hospitalization rates were lower than in the prior year, with rates for infants 0–11 months at 790.2 per 100,000 and for children 12–23 months at 611.9 per 100,000. There were 34 RSV deaths among children reported by 35 jurisdictions from September 28, 2025, to June 30, 2026, with 19 (55.9%) having an underlying condition and 16 (47.1%) hospitalized. CDC guidance suggests that infant RSV antibodies should be given October through the end of March, with some states extending the window into April. The FDA is raising safety questions about the shots, but no safety issues have been identified in clinical studies or after more than 6 million babies were immunized worldwide.
Original source text (English)
Parents walk their children -wearing face masks- to the Sagrado Corazon school on Santiago on June 14, 2023, as Chile reinstated the use of masks in schools due to a strong outbreak of a respiratory virus. Students in Chile must once again use masks in schools as a preventive measure against the "largest" outbreak of the respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) since the country has registered it, the Ministry of Health announced on June 13. (Photo by Pablo VERA / AFP) PABLO VERA/AFP via Getty Images CDC's Nov. 19 Morbidity and Mortality Weekly Report shows the 2025–26 US RSV season peaked the week ending Feb. 28 and ended in the week ending April 25, 25 days after the March 31 close of the agency's standard window for infant antibody shots . For parents of babies due this winter, the season's lower overall hospitalization rate of 97.3 per 100,000 matters less than whether the antibody dose lands before the virus arrives, because CDC's own 2026–27 season outlook says the late peak may repeat. The 97.3 rate sits just under the 99.02 to 132.9 range of 2022 through 2025, and the report says RSV severity was not higher than in recent seasons. The report covers weeks ending July 5, 2025 through June 27, 2026, with most data as of July 22, 2026 and pediatric death counts as of Aug. 11. RSV Peak Came 69 Days Later Than in 2024–25 and Activity Ended 27 Days Later CDC's national laboratory data put RSV season onset in the week ending Nov. 29, 2025, the peak share of positive tests at 9.0% in the week ending Feb. 28, 2026, and offset in the week ending April 25. CDC's prior-year report put the 2024–25 milestones at Nov. 9, 2024, a peak of 11.0% positivity on Dec. 21, 2024, and offset on March 29, 2025. Milestone (week ending) 2024–25 2025–26 Difference Season onset Nov. 9, 2024 Nov. 29, 2025 20 days later Peak percent positive Dec. 21, 2024 (11.0%) Feb. 28, 2026 (9.0%) 69 days later Season offset March 29, 2025 April 25, 2026 27 days later The differences align each 2024–25 date to the same point in the following calendar year; both sets of dates come from CDC's laboratory surveillance, and the 2025–26 report does not itself make the year-over-year comparison. CDC's hospital-based outlook tells a matching story with a different metric: the peak weekly RSV hospital admission rate was 3.0 per 100,000 in the week ending Feb. 21, 2026, about 23% below the 3.9 per 100,000 of the week ending Jan. 4, 2025. CDC's 2026–27 outlook, published Sept. 23, calls the reason for the delay uncertain, lists post-pandemic population immunity and changes in age-group transmission tied to infant immunization among possible factors, and says the later peak "may signal" another late peak this season. Infant hospitalization rates were lower than in CDC's prior-year report. RSV-NET put infants 0–11 months at 790.2 per 100,000, against 1,116.7 in the 2024–25 report ; children 12–23 months at 611.9 against 770.6; and adults 75 and older at 352.1 against 426.9. A second network, NVSN, put infants 0–11 months at 955.9 per 100,000 and children 12–23 months at 605.4; the two networks use different catchment areas and methods, so their numbers are not interchangeable. The report does not say why rates fell and offers no analysis crediting immunization products. When Does RSV Season End, and Until When Can Babies Get Antibody Shots? CDC's guidance says infant RSV antibodies should be given October through the end of March in most of the continental US, within one week of birth for babies born in that window and ideally in the birth hospital. The same page says public health authorities may issue revised timing based on local epidemiology and that providers should check with state or territorial health departments before systematically changing the months. The MMWR states that most US jurisdictions extended antibody use into April but does not say why. State health departments gave their own reasons. Washington extended infant immunization through April 30 in a March 17 announcement; State Health Officer Dr. Tao Sheng Kwan-Gett said the goal was to keep babies out of the hospital, and the state's infant hospitalization rate peaked at about 26 per 100,000 in January. Massachusetts advised extending infant immunizations in late March, saying its season began later than usual and RSV was circulating later into the spring than typical. New York City also extended through April 30 because of atypical RSV activity . Nebraska let its extension lapse on April 30 and said fall dosing would give infants a better window of protection in 2026–27. Trials of nirsevimab measured efficacy through 150 days after the shot. A dose given Oct. 1, the first day of CDC's window, reaches day 150 on Feb. 28, 2026, the week RSV positivity peaked. Protection does not end on day 150, and Washington's health department describes it as lasting at least five months. The CDC report does not analyze when infants were dosed relative to their illness; the arithmetic shows only how the calendar and the trial endpoint line up. Published 2026–27 guidance has not moved the window. Maine CDC's 2026–27 recommendations keep October 1 through March 31 for infants born in the season. The American Academy of Pediatrics left its first-season recommendation unchanged in a Sept. 2 policy statement and widened the high-risk criteria for children ages 8 through 19 months entering a second season. AAP's Dr. Sean O'Leary said the federal Advisory Committee on Immunization Practices was effectively in limbo and not meeting , leaving no new federal schedule changes. Only 3 of 17 Age-Eligible Infants Who Died of RSV Had Received an Antibody Shot CDC counted 34 RSV deaths among children reported by 35 jurisdictions for deaths occurring Sept. 28, 2025 through June 30, 2026. Nineteen (55.9%) had an underlying condition, 16 (47.1%) were hospitalized, and the median age was 1 year, with a range of 1 month to 17 years. Among 17 age-eligible infants, meaning those younger than 8 months on or after Oct. 1, 2025, 3 had received nirsevimab or clesrovimab, 11 had received neither, and 3 had unknown status. Seventeen is a small number, and the report gives no antibody or maternal-vaccine uptake figures, so it does not turn the count into an effectiveness estimate. CDC says pediatric death counts are provisional and likely undercounted because national reporting began in March 2026. FDA Review of RSV Antibodies Draws Objection From Pediatric Infectious Diseases Society News reports in December 2025 said the FDA had opened a safety review of both infant antibodies: nirsevimab (Beyfortus, from Sanofi and AstraZeneca) and clesrovimab (Enflonsia, from Merck). CIDRAP, summarizing Reuters, reported that senior FDA adviser Tracy Beth Hoeg began raising safety questions internally in June 2025 and that Health Secretary Robert F. Kennedy Jr. had raised concerns about possible risks, including seizures; the seizure claim was apparently first made by an Australian journalist. HHS spokesperson Andrew Nixon said the FDA reviews emerging data for all products and will update labeling if the evidence warrants. Sanofi said no safety issue had been identified in clinical studies or after more than 6 million babies were immunized worldwide, and cited more than 400,000 infants in over 50 studies; Merck said the bar for establishing safety is exceptionally high. The Pediatric Infectious Diseases Society said on Dec. 12 that the FDA's announcement was unusual and risked generating doubt among parents, because trials and post-licensure monitoring had shown no concerning safety signals. The National Foundation for Infectious Diseases' Dr. Kevin Ault said the FDA's request for more data was inconsistent with prior operations. The CDC report does not evaluate antibody safety, and this article did not find a published outcome of the FDA review. CDC Found No RSV Mutations Linked to Antibody Resistance but Noted Three Nirsevimab-Site Changes Both antibodies target RSV's fusion (F) protein . Because a monoclonal antibody binds one site, a single change there can matter, so CDC checked sequences from the season. Of 630 typed specimens from 16 jurisdictions, 64.8% were RSV-A, 32.4% were RSV-B and 2.9% were both. CDC found three substitutions at the nirsevimab binding site in RSV-B (I206M, Q209R, S211N), which it described as ongoing evolution, and clesrovimab-site substitutions below 1%. No substitutions linked to reduced susceptibility to either antibody were found. Outside the US, a French study of the 2024–25 season, when RSV-B predominated, found resistance-associated substitutions in 12.5% of RSV-B breakthrough infections among nirsevimab-treated infants versus 1.0% of RSV-A, and none in non-immunized infants . A February 2026 preprint co-authored by Jesse Bloom offers a biophysical explanation for why such changes are more common in RSV-B than RSV-A. Separately, Fred Hutch reported a September Nature paper analyzing more than 60,000 RSV sequences that found no sustained spread of resistance mutations to either antibody. COVID Hospitalization Rate for Adults 75 and Older Fell to 361.2 per 100,000 From 932.6 a Year Earlier COVID-NET's highest 2025–26 hospitalization rates were 361.2 per 100,000 for adults 75 and older, 173.3 for infants younger than 6 months, 111.4 for adults 65–74 and 67.3 for children 6–23 months. CDC's prior-year report listed 932.6, 285.6, 274.4 and 104.1 for the same groups. The new report does not make that comparison and the reporting periods differ. CDC's outlook evaluation puts the season's peak weekly COVID hospitalization rate at 2.0 per 100,000 in the week ending Jan. 3, 2026, against 4.2 in the week ending Jan. 4, 2025, and says its scenario modeling overestimated the size of the peak. SARS-CoV-2 test positivity peaked at 12.1% in the week ending Aug. 23, 2025, fell to 2.9% in early November, rose to 5.3% in the week ending Jan. 24, 2026 and bottomed at 0.8% at the end of May, with overall positivity of 4.4%. CDC estimates 4.1 to 12.4 million illnesses, 130,000 to 240,000 hospitalizations and 14,000 to 41,000 deaths from Oct. 1, 2025 through June 20, 2026. Provisional data show 31 pediatric COVID deaths reported by 43 jurisdictions, 74.2% with an underlying condition and a median age of 9 months. Among 20 who were old enough to be vaccinated, none had received a COVID vaccine and status was unknown for two. Federal and pediatric guidance diverge on that age group. The American Academy of Pediatrics' 2026–27 COVID recommendations, released Sept. 2, keep routine vaccination for ages 6 to 23 months. In 2025, CDC moved healthy children to shared decision-making after Kennedy announced it would no longer recommend the shot for them. BA.3.2 Reached 8% of US Sequences by June 6, Up From 0.55% in March, With a Wide Margin of Error CDC analyzed 29,023 SARS-CoV-2 sequences from July 6, 2025 through June 6, 2026 and concluded no major strain replacement occurred, the second consecutive year of that finding. JN.1 descendants dominated, and XFG was the only lineage above 50% prevalence, with its descendant XFG.1.1 growing in 2026. The exception is BA.3.2, which is not a JN.1 descendant. It carries 70–75 spike substitutions and deletions relative to JN.1 and LP.8.1 and made up 8% of sequences (95% confidence interval 4% to 17%) in the four weeks ending June 6; the interval is wide because CDC had limited specimens. In a March 19 MMWR, CDC traced BA.3.2's earliest sample to South Africa, collected Nov. 22, 2024, reported its first US clinical detection on Jan. 5, 2026, and put it at 0.55% of 5,238 US sequences collected through March 12. That report's laboratory work found the 2025–26 LP.8.1-adapted mRNA vaccine produced the lowest neutralization against BA.3.2 among seven variants tested, and the authors said the effect on health outcomes remained to be determined. CDC's December outlook raised the possibility that BA.3.2 or a descendant could replace JN.1 lineages and lift the winter hospitalization peak; as of May 4, CDC said BA.3.2 was not the predominant variant . The 2026–27 COVID vaccines target XFG, and the new report does not evaluate how they perform against BA.3.2. Adults 75 and Older and Some Adults 50 to 74 Should Get One RSV Vaccine Dose, CDC Says CDC recommends that infants in their first RSV season be protected either by maternal Abrysvo vaccination in September through January or by nirsevimab or clesrovimab in October through March, with jurisdictions free to adjust the timing. A single RSV vaccine dose is recommended for adults 75 and older and for adults 50 to 74 at increased risk. COVID antivirals remain underused, and CDC lists vaccination, hand hygiene, ventilation and staying home when sick as practices for everyone. The lower 97.3 per 100,000 RSV hospitalization rate does not mean the calendar worked as designed. The season peaked 69 days later than the year before, ended after CDC's standard antibody window, and CDC cannot yet say why; its outlook warns the pattern may recur. Parents of babies due or born near the edges of October through March should ask their pediatrician or birthing hospital about antibody and maternal-vaccine timing and check their state health department's current guidance. Adults 75 and older, and some 50 to 74, should ask about the one-time RSV vaccine, and the XFG-targeted COVID vaccines remain the main variant-specific tool as BA.3.2 stays under CDC watch. Frequently Asked Questions Can a baby born outside the October-to-March window still get an RSV antibody? Yes. The American Academy of Pediatrics says infants younger than 8 months born before RSV season should receive the immunization rather than wait. CDC says individual providers may use clinical judgment to give antibodies outside October through March. Was the 2025–26 RSV season milder, or only later? Both, in part. The overall hospitalization rate was just under the prior range, the report says severity was not higher than in recent seasons, and the peak weekly hospital admission rate was about 23% below the prior season's. The timing, however, ran about 10 weeks behind last season's peak, and CDC says the reason is uncertain and a later peak may repeat in 2026–27. Can RSV mutate around antibody shots? It can in principle, because each antibody binds a single site on the F protein. CDC found three nirsevimab-site changes in RSV-B and none linked to reduced susceptibility, while a French study of 2024–25 breakthrough infections found resistance-associated changes in 12.5% of RSV-B cases, all in treated infants. Do the 2026–27 COVID vaccines cover BA.3.2? The 2026–27 vaccines target XFG, a JN.1-lineage variant, while BA.3.2 is a separate lineage with 70–75 spike changes. Laboratory data showed reduced neutralization of BA.3.2 by antibodies from the 2025–26 vaccine, but CDC says the real-world health impact remains to be determined

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

尼塞韦单抗 简介 德国主要儿科中心的婴儿 RSV 住院治疗减少了一半 - Bioengineer.org

Nirsevimab Introduction Halved Infant RSV Hospitalizations at Major German Pediatric Center - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报一项在德国夏里蒂大学医学院柏林儿童医院进行的研究发现,引入尼塞韦单抗单克隆抗体后,2024–2025赛季婴儿呼吸道合胞病毒(RSV)住院病例减少了51%。这一显著下降是在2024年德国疾病预防控制中心(STIKO)发布被动免疫接种的全国性建议之后出现的。研究分析了2018–2019、2023–2024以及2024–2025三个流行季节中261名两岁以下住院儿童的数据,结果显示,在2024–2025赛季因RSV住院的婴儿中,有90.2%未接受该抗体。研究还指出,夏里蒂中心在2023–2024年柏林都市区RSV相关住院病例中占比33%,在2024–2025年占比25.6%,与罗伯特·科赫研究所报告的全国范围内55%的下降趋势一致。研究结果表明,尼塞韦单抗的使用与急性医疗服务需求的减少存在显著相关性,但报告也承认在解释这些结果时存在方法学上的局限性。该研究突显了RSV对儿科医疗系统造成的显著季节性负担,并强调了预防性措施的潜在影响。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究在 2018 年至 2025 年的三个流行季节进行,其中 2024 年至 2025 年是第一个根据系统公共卫生建议普遍预防婴儿 RSV 的季节。报告发布日期为2026年11月19日。
地点与对象
这项研究是在柏林夏里特大学儿童医院进行的,主要针对两岁以下的婴儿。该分析包括来自柏林大都市区的儿童,该医院占 2023-2024 年与 RSV 相关住院的 33%,2024-2025 年占 25.6%。
事件
柏林夏里特大学儿童医院的一项回顾性观察调查显示,在全国范围内实施 呼吸道合胞病毒 被动免疫后,因 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 住院的儿童人数减少了 51%。该研究还指出,2024-2025 年季节期间,超过 90% 的住院婴儿没有接受过 尼塞韦单抗。
规模、影响与风险
与 2018-2019 年季节相比,三级中心的 RSV 住院人数下降了 44%,与 2023-2024 年季节相比下降了 51%。2019 年至 2022 年间,德国记录了近十万名儿童住院治疗,全国范围内的减少量估计为 55%。该研究强调了 RSV 对儿科医疗基础设施造成的重大季节性压力。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究是一项回顾性观察调查,其结果基于医院记录和通过聚合酶链反应或快速抗原检测进行的实验室确认。研究结果得到了罗伯特·科赫研究所和联邦卫生部的支持,但报告指出了方法上的限制,限制了对研究结果的直接解释。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Nirsevimab Introduction Halved Infant RSV Hospitalizations at Major German Pediatric Center - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A study conducted at the Children’s Hospital of Charité – Universitätsklinikum Berlin in Germany found that the introduction of nirsevimab, a monoclonal antibody, led to a 51% reduction in infant RSV hospitalizations during the 2024–2025 season. This marked decrease followed the national recommendation for passive immunization issued by the Standing Committee on Vaccination (STIKO) in 2024. The research analyzed 261 hospitalized children under two years of age across three epidemic seasons (2018–2019, 2023–2024, and 2024–2025), revealing that 90.2% of infants hospitalized with RSV during the 2024–2025 season had not received the antibody. The study also noted that the Charité center accounted for 33% of RSV-related hospitalizations in the Berlin metropolitan area during 2023–2024 and 25.6% during 2024–2025, aligning with the nationwide 55% reduction reported by the Robert Koch Institute. The findings suggest a strong correlation between nirsevimab uptake and reduced acute healthcare utilization, though the report acknowledges methodological limitations in interpreting the results. The study underscores the significant seasonal burden of RSV on pediatric healthcare systems and highlights the potential impact of prophylactic measures.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Wednesday, November 25, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Biology Nirsevimab Introduction Halved Infant RSV Hospitalizations at Major German Pediatric Center by November 19, 2026 in Biology Reading Time: 5 mins read 0 Nirsevimab Introduction Halved Infant RSV Hospitalizations at Major German Pediatric Center Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A retrospective observational investigation conducted at the Children’s Hospital of Charité – Universitätsklinikum Berlin demonstrated that pediatric hospital admissions associated with respiratory syncytial virus fell by fifty-one percent following the nationwide implementation of broad passive immunization with the monoclonal antibody nirsevimab. Analyzing young children under two years of age admitted across three separate epidemic seasons between 2018 and 2025, researchers documented a marked contraction in inpatient volume during the 2024–2025 season, which constituted Germany’s inaugural period under systematic public health recommendations for universal infant RSV prophylaxis. Concurrently, the investigation revealed that more than ninety percent of the pediatric patients hospitalized with confirmed infections during this initial intervention season had not received nirsevimab, underscoring the real-world connection between prophylactic uptake and acute secondary healthcare utilization. Respiratory syncytial virus represents a principal global driver of severe lower respiratory tract morbidity, acute hospitalization, and recurrent clinical interventions among neonates, infants, and young children. Within the German healthcare framework alone, documented epidemiological figures reflect almost one hundred thousand pediatric hospitalizations attributable to the pathogen between 2019 and 2022, exerting extreme seasonal strain on specialized inpatient wards and pediatric intensive care infrastructure. In temperate climatic regions such as Germany, viral transmission follows a defined seasonal pattern spanning October through May, during which pediatric facilities regularly encounter steep admission surges driven by acute bronchiolitis and pneumonia. Addressing this substantial clinical footprint has long required reliable prophylactic options capable of shielding vulnerable infant populations throughout the winter epidemic wave. Following European Union regulatory approval of nirsevimab in 2022, national immunization policy in Germany shifted substantially in 2024 when the Standing Committee on Vaccination, known as STIKO and based at the Robert Koch Institute, issued a formal recommendation for passive immunization. The policy established routine nirsevimab administration for all infants entering or born during their first RSV season, alongside targeted coverage for children up to twenty-four months of age who possess underlying medical comorbidities predisposing them to severe disease trajectories. Critically, immunization costs were integrated into standard statutory healthcare coverage. Early epidemiological surveillance from the Robert Koch Institute and the Federal Ministry of Health subsequently pointed toward a nationwide fifty-five percent decrease in infant hospital admissions during the 2024–2025 season relative to the preceding cycle. Discover more Save on Rx Find Lung Care Track Biotech respiratory syncytial virus Respiratory syncytial virus To evaluate these population-level signals within a detailed tertiary clinical environment, the research team performed a single-center retrospective cohort analysis evaluating two hundred and sixty-one hospitalized children under two years of age across three defined epidemiological intervals: the pre-pandemic 2018–2019 season, the pre-intervention 2023–2024 season, and the post-recommendation 2024–2025 season. During the total surveillance window, three hundred and forty pediatric patients presented with documented RSV infections, but seventy-nine individuals were excluded from the primary analytical cohort due to pervasive missingness across demographic, clinical, or diagnostic parameters. The final analyzed sample captured children presenting throughout the cold-weather transmission window, representing a significant proportion of tertiary pediatric respiratory admissions in the Berlin metropolitan territory. Diagnostic verification required laboratory confirmation via polymerase chain reaction or rapid antigen testing performed as part of routine emergency intake. While most admissions stemmed from hypoxemia and respiratory distress, other recorded clinical indications included dehydration, poor feeding, and systemic observation for persistent fever. Across the entire analyzed cohort, common presenting symptoms included cough in seventy-eight point five percent of patients and tachypnea in sixty-seven point eight percent, while clinically significant apnea was documented in one point one percent of cases. Males formed a consistent slight majority across all evaluated years, accounting for fifty-eight point two percent of admissions. Discover more Check Symptoms Read Med Journals Find Family Lawyer The medical severity of inpatient admissions was characterized through detailed clinical support records, with seventy-four point three percent of all admitted patients across the three seasons requiring supplemental oxygen therapy. Conventional low-flow nasal cannula administration was utilized in sixty-five point one percent of cases, whereas high-flow nasal cannula therapy was deployed in twenty-four point one percent of children exhibiting persistent respiratory distress. Invasive mechanical ventilation was not required for any child in the cohort following the 2018–2019 season, and zero inpatient mortalities occurred over the entirety of the study. Prematurity represented the most frequent pre-existing medical risk factor, recorded in fifteen point three percent of patients, followed by chronic lung disease, with comorbid baseline prevalence remaining stable across comparative seasonal cohorts. Quantitative analysis demonstrated that following the implementation of the STIKO recommendation, RSV hospitalizations in the tertiary center dropped markedly compared to both the 2018–2019 season, yielding a hospitalization count ratio of 0.56 with a 95 percent confidence interval of 0.40 to 0.78, and the 2023–2024 season, yielding a count ratio of 0.49 with a 95 percent confidence interval of 0.35 to 0.68. These estimates corresponded to relative decreases of approximately forty-four percent and fifty-one percent, respectively. Strikingly, ninety point two percent of all children hospitalized with RSV during the 2024–2025 season were unimmunized, and the overall immunization rate among eligible hospitalized patients was only seven point four percent. Furthermore, none of the high-risk children older than twelve months who met comorbidity criteria for second-season prophylaxis had received the antibody prior to admission. The investigators contextualized these institutional trends within regional and international epidemiological benchmarks. The Charité center accounted for thirty-three percent of all RSV-related hospitalizations in this pediatric age group across the Berlin metropolitan area in 2023–2024, and twenty-five point six percent during 2024–2025, confirming that local trends closely mirrored the nationwide fifty-five percent hospital reduction reported by the Robert Koch Institute. When contrasted with international cohorts, however, Germany’s fifty-one percent institutional reduction appeared more modest than findings from Italian observational studies, where universal immunization programs achieved coverage rates around ninety percent and drove eighty to eighty-six percent reductions in pediatric RSV admissions. The authors reasoned that differing population-level uptake explains these discrepancies, emphasizing that maximal reductions in hospital burden depend heavily on achieving widespread immunization coverage. Several critical methodological constraints limit the direct interpretation of the observed findings. Because national guidelines prioritized infants aged twelve months or younger, thirty-one point five percent of the study’s admitted cohort was ineligible for routine nirsevimab based on age alone. Furthermore, the retrospective, monocentric design lacked a defined population denominator, preventing statistical calculation of formal vaccine effectiveness, relative risk reductions, or community-wide incidence rates. The reliance on absolute admission counts left observations vulnerable to temporal variations in hospital referral networks, shifting admission criteria, and natural viral circulation dynamics. Future research directions highlighted by the authors include evaluating the durability of passive immunity beyond six months, assessing long-term immune responses following attenuated infections, and appraising maternal immunization as a complementary strategy to reduce infant disease burdens globally. View five news elements
Time
The study was conducted across three epidemic seasons between 2018 and 2025, with the 2024–2025 season being the first under systematic public health recommendations for universal infant RSV prophylaxis. The publication date of the report is November 19, 2026.
Location and population
The study was carried out at the Children’s Hospital of Charité – Universitätsklinikum Berlin, focusing on infants under two years of age. The analysis included children from the Berlin metropolitan area, with the hospital accounting for 33% of RSV-related hospitalizations in 2023–2024 and 25.6% in 2024–2025.
Event
A retrospective observational investigation at the Children’s Hospital of Charité – Universitätsklinikum Berlin showed that pediatric hospital admissions for respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) decreased by fifty-one percent following the nationwide implementation of passive immunization with nirsevimab. The study also noted that more than ninety percent of hospitalized infants during the 2024–2025 season had not received nirsevimab.
Scale, impact and risk
RSV hospitalizations in the tertiary center dropped by 44% compared to the 2018–2019 season and 51% compared to the 2023–2024 season. The nationwide reduction was estimated at 55%, with almost one hundred thousand pediatric hospitalizations documented in Germany between 2019 and 2022. The study highlights the significant seasonal strain on pediatric healthcare infrastructure due to RSV.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study was conducted as a retrospective observational investigation, with findings based on hospital records and laboratory confirmation via polymerase chain reaction or rapid antigen testing. The results are supported by the Robert Koch Institute and the Federal Ministry of Health, but the report notes methodological constraints that limit direct interpretation of the findings.
